for each
\alpha\in\lambda
and
(s,g)\leq(s_{\alpha},g_{\alpha})
, where
s=\bigcup_{\alpha\in\lambda}s_{\alpha}
. Let
f^{\mathbb{D}_{\kappa}}:{}^{<\kappa}({}^{<\kappa}\kappa)\to{}^{<\kappa}\kappa
send
\left\langle s_{\alpha}\mid\alpha\in\lambda\right\rangle\mapsto\bigcup_{\alpha\in\lambda}s_{\alpha}
(assuming this is well-defined, and arbitrary otherwise), then
f^{\mathbb{D}_{\kappa}}
witnesses that
\mathbb{D}_{\kappa}
is
\kappa
-centred with canonical bounds.
∎We are now ready to give proofs for the values of the
\mathfrak{b}
-side cardinals in the
\kappa
-Hechler model and the
\mathfrak{d}
-side cardinals in the dual
\kappa
-Hechler model.

Theorem 5.8

Let
\mathbb{P}=\langle\mathbb{P}_{\alpha},\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}\mid\alpha\in\kappa^{++}\rangle
be a
{<}\kappa
-support iteration, where
\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}
is a
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}
-name for the
\kappa
-Hechler forcing
\mathbb{D}_{\kappa}
, let
\mathbf{V}\vDash\text{``}\,2^{\kappa}=\kappa^{+}\,\text{''}
and let
G
be
\mathbb{P}
-generic over
\mathbf{V}
. For any
b,h\in{}^{\kappa}\kappa
(including new functions) such that
h\leq^{*}\mathrm{cf}(b)
we have:

\displaystyle\mathbf{V}[G]

\displaystyle\vDash\text{``}\,\mathfrak{b}_{\kappa}^{b,h}(\in^{*})=\mathfrak{b}_{\kappa}^{b}(\leq^{*})=\mathfrak{b}_{\kappa}^{b,h}(\mathrel{\cancel{\ni^{\infty}}})=\mathfrak{b}_{\kappa}^{b}(\mathrel{\cancel{=^{\infty}}})=\kappa^{+}\,\text{''}

It suffices to prove that
\mathbf{V}[G]\vDash\text{``}\,\mathfrak{b}_{\kappa}^{b}(\mathrel{\cancel{=^{\infty}}})=\kappa^{+}\,\text{''}
. Let
b\in({}^{\kappa}\kappa)^{\mathbf{V}[G]}
, then there exists some
\alpha\in\kappa^{++}
such that
b\in({}^{\kappa}\kappa)^{\mathbf{V}[G_{\alpha}]}
. Note that
\mathbf{V}[G_{\alpha}]\vDash\text{``}\,2^{\kappa}=\kappa^{+}\,\text{''}
holds for any
\alpha<\kappa^{++}
, and thus
\mathbf{V}[G]\vDash\text{``}\,|(\prod b)^{\mathbf{V}[G_{\alpha}]}|=\kappa^{+}\,\text{''}
. We will show that
(\prod b)^{\mathbf{V}[G_{\alpha}]}
is a witness for
\mathbf{V}[G]\vDash\text{``}\,\mathfrak{b}_{\kappa}^{b}(\mathrel{\cancel{=^{\infty}}})=\kappa^{+}\,\text{''}
. We will assume for notational convenience that in fact
b\in\mathbf{V}
.

Let
\dot{f}
be a
\mathbb{P}
-name such that
\Vdash_{\mathbb{P}}\text{``}\,\dot{f}\in\prod b\,\text{''}
, and let
f\in\mathbf{V}[G]
be the function named by
\dot{f}
. Then there is some
\mu<\kappa^{++}
such that
f\in\mathbf{V}[G_{\mu}]
, and thus we can assume without loss of generality that
\dot{f}
is a
\mathbb{P}_{\mu}
-name. Since
\mathbf{V}\vDash\text{``}\,\mu<(2^{\kappa})^{+}=\kappa^{++}\,\text{''}
, we see that
\mathbb{P}_{\mu}
is
\kappa
-centred by
5.6
. Therefore there exists
h\in(\prod b)^{\mathbf{V}}
such that
\Vdash_{\mathbb{P}_{\mu}}\text{``}\,\dot{f}=^{\infty}h\,\text{''}
, as was needed.
∎

Theorem 5.9

Let
\mathbb{P}=\langle\mathbb{P}_{\alpha},\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}\mid\alpha\in\kappa^{++}\cdot\kappa^{+}\rangle
be a
{<}\kappa
-support iteration, where
\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}
is a
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}
-name for the
\kappa
-Hechler forcing
\mathbb{D}_{\kappa}
, let
\mathbf{V}\vDash\text{``}\,2^{\kappa}=\kappa^{+}\,\text{''}
and let
G
be
\mathbb{P}
-generic over
\mathbf{V}
. For any
b,h\in{}^{\kappa}\kappa
(including new functions) such that
h\leq^{*}\mathrm{cf}(b)
we have:

\displaystyle\mathbf{V}[G]

\displaystyle\vDash\text{``}\,\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b,h}(\in^{*})=\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b}(\leq^{*})=\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b,h}(\mathrel{\cancel{\ni^{\infty}}})=\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b}(\mathrel{\cancel{=^{\infty}}})=\kappa^{++}\,\text{''}

It suffices to prove that
\mathbf{V}[G]\vDash\text{``}\,\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b}(\mathrel{\cancel{=^{\infty}}})=\kappa^{++}\,\text{''}
. Let
b\in({}^{\kappa}\kappa)^{\mathbf{V}[G]}
, then there exists some
\alpha\in\kappa^{++}\cdot\kappa^{+}
such that
b\in({}^{\kappa}\kappa)^{\mathbf{V}[G_{\alpha}]}
. Let
\beta=\alpha+\kappa^{++}
, then
\beta<\kappa^{++}\cdot\kappa^{+}
and
\mathbf{V}[G_{\beta}]\vDash\text{``}\,\mathrm{add}(\mathcal{M}_{\kappa})=\kappa^{++}=2^{\kappa}\,\text{''}
by similar arguments as those given for the
\kappa
-Hechler model. This implies particularly that
\mathbf{V}[G_{\beta}]\vDash\text{``}\,\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b}(\mathrel{\cancel{=^{\infty}}})=\kappa^{++}=2^{\kappa}\,\text{''}
as well. We will work in
\mathbf{V}[G_{\beta}]
as our new ground model. Let
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}=\mathbb{P}/G_{\beta}\in\mathbf{V}[G_{\beta}]
be the quotient forcing and
H
be
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
-generic such that
\mathbf{V}[G]=\mathbf{V}[G_{\beta}][H]
. Note that
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
is itself (equivalent to) a
{<}\kappa
-support iteration of
\mathbb{D}_{\kappa}
of length
\kappa^{++}\cdot\kappa^{+}
.

Since
\mathbf{V}[G_{\beta}]\vDash\text{``}\,2^{\kappa}=\kappa^{++}\,\text{''}
, we see that
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
is
\kappa
-centred by
5.6
. Let
\{\dot{f}_{\alpha}\mid\alpha\in\kappa^{+}\}
be a set of names such that
\Vdash_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}\text{``}\,\dot{f}_{\alpha}\in\prod b\,\text{''}
. Using
5.4
, we can find
\left\{g_{\alpha}^{\gamma}\mid\gamma\in\kappa\right\}\subseteq\prod b
in
\mathbf{V}[G_{\beta}]
such that for any
h\in\prod b
with
h=^{\infty}g_{\alpha}^{\gamma}
for all
\gamma\in\kappa
, we also have
\Vdash_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}\text{``}\,h=^{\infty}\dot{f}_{\alpha}\,\text{''}
. Finally, since
\mathbf{V}[G_{\beta}]\vDash\text{``}\,\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b}(\mathrel{\cancel{=^{\infty}}})=\kappa^{++}\,\text{''}
, we see that
|\left\{g_{\alpha}^{\gamma}\mid\alpha\in\kappa^{+}\land\gamma\in\kappa\right\}|<\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b}(\mathrel{\cancel{=^{\infty}}})
, and therefore there exists
h\in\prod b
such that
h=^{\infty}g_{\alpha}^{\gamma}
for all
\alpha\in\kappa^{+}
and
\gamma\in\kappa
. Hence
\Vdash_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}\text{``}\,h=^{\infty}\dot{f}_{\alpha}\,\text{''}
for each
\alpha\in\kappa^{+}
, showing that
\mathbf{V}[G]\vDash\text{``}\,\kappa^{+}=|\{\dot{f}_{\alpha}\mid\alpha\in\kappa^{+}\}|<\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b}(\mathrel{\cancel{=^{\infty}}})=\kappa^{++}\,\text{''}
.
∎

5.3. Separating Cardinals of the Form
\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b,h}(\in^{*})

It was first shown in
[
BBTFM18
]
that
\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{\mathsf{pow}}(\in^{*})<\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{\mathsf{id}}(\in^{*})
is consistent, by forcing with a generalisation of Sacks forcing due to Kanamori
[
Kan80
]
. In
[
vdV23
]
, we generalised this construction using a perfect-tree forcing where the number of successors of splitting nodes is governed by a function
h
. We used this to prove that there can in fact consistently be
\kappa^{+}
many functions
h_{\xi}
such that all cardinals
\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{h_{\xi}}(\in^{*})
are mutually different.

It turns out that the same approach could also be used to produce models where cardinals of the form
\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b,h}(\in^{*})
have different values from each other. Since our forcing construction is in practice nearly identical to the construction from
[
vdV23
]
, we will state most results without proof, and instead refer to the relevant proof in the aforementioned article.

Remark 5.10

Note, that we used a different definition of
h
-slalom in this article, where the bound
\varphi(\alpha)<h(\alpha)
is strict, whereas in our previous article
h
-slaloms
\varphi
are defined such that
\varphi(\alpha)\leq h(\alpha)
. The effect of this minor change, is that we will often have to use a successor cardinality whenever
h
is used, or replace
<
by
\leq
, compared to the analogous result in
[
vdV23
]
.

The main difference between our current forcing notion and the forcing notion from
[
vdV23
]
, is that we work with trees on
\prod b
instead of trees on
{}^{\kappa}\kappa
. For many of the properties we need, this difference is irrelevant.

Definition 5.11

(Cf.
[
vdV23
]
Definition 1.1)

Let
\mathbb{S}_{\kappa}^{b,h}
be the forcing notion with conditions
T
being trees on
\prod b
such that

(i)

for any
u\in T
there exists
v\in T
such that
u\subseteq v
and
v
is splitting,

(ii)

if
u\in\mathrm{Split}_{\alpha}(T)
, then
u
is an
h(\alpha)
-splitting node in
T
,

(iii)

if
C\subseteq T
is a chain of splitting nodes with
|C|<\kappa
, then
\bigcup C
is a splitting node in
T
.

We order
\mathbb{S}_{\kappa}^{b,h}
by
T\leq S
iff

\bullet

T\subseteq S
and

\bullet

for every
u\in T
, if
\mathrm{suc}(u,T)\neq\mathrm{suc}(u,S)
then
|\mathrm{suc}(u,T)|<|\mathrm{suc}(u,S)|
.

Definition 5.12

Define an ordering by
T\leq_{\alpha}S
iff
T\leq S
and
\mathrm{Split}_{\alpha}(T)=\mathrm{Split}_{\alpha}(S)
.

It is easy to see that this is a fusion ordering.

Theorem 5.13

(Cf.
[
vdV23
]
Lemmas 1.3 and 1.7)

{\mathbb{S}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
is
{<}\kappa
-closed, closed under fusion, and
{\mathbb{S}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
has the
{<}(2^{\kappa})^{+}
-c.c.. Therefore,
{\mathbb{S}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
preserves all cardinals
{\leq}\kappa
and
{>}2^{\kappa}
and does not destroy the inaccessibility of
\kappa
.

We will use our forcing notion in ground models where
2^{\kappa}=\kappa^{+}
, thus the above gives us the preservation of all cardinals with the exception of
\kappa^{+}
. The preservation of
\kappa^{+}
will follow from
5.15
below. We need two main ingredients if we want to separate two localisation cardinals
\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b,h}(\in^{*})
and
\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b^{\prime},h^{\prime}}(\in^{*})
, using a preservation property that we will call the
(b,h)
-Sacks property.

Definition 5.14

\mathbb{P}
has the
(b,h)
-Sacks property
if for every
\mathbb{P}
-name
\dot{f}
and
p\in\mathbb{P}
such that
p\Vdash\text{``}\,\dot{f}\in\prod b\,\text{''}
there exists a
\varphi\in\mathrm{Loc}_{\kappa}^{b,h}
and
q\leq p
such that
q\Vdash\text{``}\,\dot{f}(\xi)\in\varphi(\xi)\,\text{''}
for all
\xi<\kappa
.

Hence, if
\Phi\subseteq\mathrm{Loc}_{\kappa}^{b,h}
is a witness for
\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b,h}(\in^{*})
in the ground model, then
\Phi
will still witness
\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b,h}(\in^{*})
in extensions by forcing notions with the
(b,h)
-Sacks property.

Our strategy is the following. Assuming that
b
is sufficiently larger than
h
, there is some
F\in{}^{\kappa}\kappa
such that
{\mathbb{S}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
has the
(b,F)
-Sacks property, and reversely that for any
F
there exists some
h
such that
{\mathbb{S}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
does not have the
F
-Sacks property. In other words, assuming
b
is large enough, for any
F_{0}
we may find
h
and
F_{1}
such that
{\mathbb{S}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
does not have the
(b,F_{0})
-Sacks property, but does have the
(b,F_{1})
-Sacks property. This implies that we can increase
\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b,F_{0}}(\in^{*})
while the ground model forms a witness for
\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b,F_{1}}(\in^{*})
.

As for what it means that
b
is sufficiently larger than
h
, it will be sufficient that
(2^{h})^{+}\leq b
. In this section, we will assume without mention that
b
is always sufficiently large.

We call
T\in{\mathbb{S}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
a
sharp
tree (below
\alpha
) if each
u\in\mathrm{Split}_{\xi}(T)
is a sharp
h(\xi)
-splitting node (for all
\xi<\alpha
). The set
({\mathbb{S}^{b,h}_{\kappa}})^{*}=\{T\in{\mathbb{S}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}\mid T\text{ is sharp}\}
embeds densely into
{\mathbb{S}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
, thus for each
T\in{\mathbb{S}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
we can choose some
T^{*}\leq T
such that
T^{*}
is sharp.

Theorem 5.15

(Cf.
[
vdV23
]
Theorem 1.8)

Let
F:\xi\mapsto(h(\xi)^{|\xi|})^{+}
, then
{\mathbb{S}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
has the
(b,F)
-Sacks property.

We will follow the proof of Theorem 1.8 in
[
vdV23
]
, summarised below.

We create a fusion sequence
\left\langle T_{\xi}\mid\xi\in\kappa\right\rangle
and a family of sets
\left\{B_{\xi}\mid\xi\in\kappa\right\}
such that for every
\xi\in\kappa
we have
|B_{\xi}|\leq h(\xi)^{|\xi|}<F(\xi)
and
T_{\xi+1}\Vdash\text{``}\,\dot{f}(\xi)\in B_{\xi}\,\text{''}
.

To ensure we can choose
B_{\xi}
small enough, we let each
T_{\xi}
be sharp below
\xi
. That is, we choose
T_{\xi}
such that
|\mathrm{suc}(u,T_{\xi})|=h(\alpha)
for each
u\in\mathrm{Split}_{\alpha}(T_{\xi})
with
\alpha<\xi
.

We define a sharp tree
T_{\xi}^{*}\leq T_{\xi}
(which does not remove any nodes from the
\xi
-th splitting level by the above property, since
T_{\xi}
is already sharp below
\xi
) and the set of successors of the
\xi
-th splitting level of
T_{\xi}^{*}
:

\displaystyle V_{\xi}=\bigcup\{\mathrm{suc}(u,T_{\xi}^{*})\mid u\in\mathrm{Split}_{\xi}(T_{\xi}^{*})\}

Subsequently we find
T_{\xi+1}
by pruning above the
\xi
-th splitting level such that for each
v\in V_{\xi}
the subtree
(T_{\xi+1})_{v}\Vdash\text{``}\,\dot{f}(\xi)=\beta^{v}_{\xi}\,\text{''}
for some ordinal
\beta_{\xi}^{v}
. Finally we define
B_{\xi}=\{\beta_{\xi}^{v}\mid v\in V_{\xi}\}
and note that
|B_{\xi}|\leq|V_{\xi}|\leq h(\xi)^{|\xi|}<F(\xi)
. The slalom
\varphi\in\mathrm{Loc}_{\kappa}^{b,F}
witnessing the
(b,F)
-Sacks property is defined as
\varphi:\xi\mapsto B_{\xi}
.
∎

Corollary 5.16

(cf.
[
vdV23
]
Corollary 1.9)

{\mathbb{S}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
preserves
\kappa^{+}
. If
\mathbf{V}\vDash\text{``}\,2^{\kappa}=\kappa^{+}\,\text{''}
, then
{\mathbb{S}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
preserves all cardinalities.

Theorem 5.17

(cf.
[
vdV23
]
Theorem 1.11)

If
S=\left\{\alpha\in\kappa\mid F(\alpha)\leq h(\alpha)\right\}
is stationary, then
{\mathbb{S}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
does not have the
(b,F)
-Sacks property.

Let
\dot{f}
name the generic
{\mathbb{S}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
-real in
(\prod b)^{\mathbf{V}^{{\mathbb{S}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}}}
and
\varphi\in\mathbf{V}
be a
(b,F)
-slalom. For any
\alpha_{0}\in\kappa
can find
T\in{\mathbb{S}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
and
\alpha\geq\alpha_{0}
such that
\mathrm{Split}_{\alpha}(T)=\mathrm{Lev}_{\alpha}(T)
, then each
u\in\mathrm{Split}_{\alpha}(T)
has
h(\alpha)
successors, thus we can find a successor
v\in\mathrm{suc}(u,T)
for which
v(\alpha)\notin\varphi(\alpha)
, because
|\varphi(\alpha)|<F(\alpha)\leq h(\alpha)
. Then
(T)_{v}\Vdash\text{``}\,\dot{f}(\alpha)\notin\varphi(\alpha)\,\text{''}
.
∎

The properties above are preserved under
{\leq}\kappa
-supported products of forcing notions
{\mathbb{S}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
, in the sense of the following two lemmas. In comparing our notation, we again have to take care with the discrepancy mentioned in
5.10
.

Lemma 5.18

(cf.
[
vdV23
]
Lemma 2.5)

Let
B\subseteq A
be sets of ordinals and
\left\langle h_{\xi}\mid\xi\in A\right\rangle
a sequence of functions in with
2^{h_{\xi}}\in\prod b
for each
\xi\in A
. Define
\overline{\mathbb{S}}=\prod^{\leq\kappa}_{\xi\in A}\mathbb{S}^{h_{\xi}}_{\kappa}
and let
G
be an
\overline{\mathbb{S}}
-generic filter. If
F:\alpha\mapsto((\sup_{\xi\in A\setminus B}h_{\xi}(\alpha))^{|\alpha|})^{+}
has the property that
F\leq^{*}b
, then for each
f\in(\prod b)^{\mathbf{V}[G]}
there exists some
\varphi\in(\mathrm{Loc}_{\kappa}^{b,F})^{\mathbf{V}[G\restriction B]}
such that
f\in^{*}\varphi
.

This proof is a product version of
5.15
and follows the construction made in the proof of Lemma 2.5 of
[
vdV23
]
. We give a summary of the construction below.

We create a generalised fusion sequence
\left\langle(p_{\xi},Z_{\xi})\mid\xi\in\kappa\right\rangle
with
p_{\xi}\in\overline{\mathbb{S}}
and
\dot{D}_{\xi}
naming sets of ordinals
D_{\xi}\in\mathbf{V}[G\restriction B]
with
|D_{\xi}|<F(\xi)
such that
p_{\xi+1}\Vdash\text{``}\,\dot{f}(\xi)\in\dot{D}_{\xi}\,\text{''}
.

We assume that
|Z_{\xi}|\leq|\xi|
and for each
\beta\in Z_{\xi}
we ensure
p_{\xi}(\beta)
is sharp below
\xi
. We can define the set of successor nodes of the
\xi
-th splitting level for each
\beta\in Z_{\xi}
as:

\displaystyle V_{\xi}^{\beta}=\bigcup\left\{\mathrm{suc}(u,p_{\xi}(\beta))\mid u\in\mathrm{Split}_{\xi}(p_{\xi}(\beta))\right\}

For each function
g:Z_{\xi}\to\bigcup_{\beta\in Z_{\xi}}V_{\xi}^{\beta}
picking an element
g(\beta)\in V_{\xi}^{\beta}
for each
\beta\in Z_{\xi}
, we will decide (a name for) a value of
\dot{f}(\xi)
. Since our sets of ordinals
D_{\xi}
are elements of
\mathbf{V}[G\restriction B]
, we only care about the part of the name decided by the support outside of
B
, that is, in
Z_{\xi}\setminus B
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\xi}

\displaystyle=\left\{g:Z_{\xi}\to\textstyle\bigcup_{\beta\in Z_{\xi}}V_{\xi}^{\beta}\mid g(\beta)\in V_{\xi}^{\beta}\text{ for all }\beta\in Z_{\xi}\right\}

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\xi}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\left\{g\restriction(Z_{\xi}\setminus B)\mid g\in\mathcal{V}_{\xi}\right\}

Because
|\mathcal{V}_{\xi}^{\prime}|\leq F(\xi)
, we can describe a name
\dot{D}_{\xi}
that only depends on the support in
B
(and thus names an element of
\mathbf{V}[G\restriction B]
) such that

\displaystyle p_{\xi+1}\Vdash\text{``}\,\dot{f}(\xi)\in\dot{D}_{\xi}\text{ and }|\dot{D}_{\xi}|\leq F(\xi)\,\text{''}

We refer to
[
vdV23
]
for a detailed construction of such names. If
p
is the limit of the generalised fusion sequence
\left\langle(p_{\xi},Z_{\xi})\mid\xi\in\kappa\right\rangle
, then we finally define the name
\dot{\varphi}
such that
p\Vdash\text{``}\,\dot{\varphi}(\xi)=\dot{D}_{\xi}\,\text{''}
. Then
\dot{\varphi}
is a name for a
(b,F)
-slalom in
\mathbf{V}[G\restriction B]
localising the function
f\in\mathbf{V}[G]
.
∎

Lemma 5.19

(cf.
[
vdV23
]
Lemma 2.7)

Let
B\subseteq A
be sets of ordinals and
\left\langle h_{\xi}\mid\xi\in A\right\rangle
a sequence of functions in with
2^{h_{\xi}}\in\prod b
for each
\xi\in A
. Define
\overline{\mathbb{S}}=\prod^{\leq\kappa}_{\xi\in A}\mathbb{S}^{b,h_{\xi}}_{\kappa}
and let
G
be an
\overline{\mathbb{S}}
-generic filter. Let
S_{\xi}=\left\{\alpha\in\kappa\mid F(\alpha)\leq h_{\xi}(\alpha)\right\}
be stationary for each
\xi\in B
, then
\mathbf{V}[G]\vDash\text{``}\,|B|\leq\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b,F}(\in^{*})\,\text{''}
.

We give a summary of the proof of Lemma 2.7 in
[
vdV23
]
.

Given a family
\Phi=\left\{\varphi_{\xi}\mid\xi\in\mu\right\}\subseteq\mathrm{Loc}_{\kappa}^{b,F}
in
\mathbf{V}[G]
with
\mu<|B|
, we can find
A^{\prime}\subseteq A
with
|A^{\prime}|=\kappa^{+}\cdot\mu
such that
\Phi\in\mathbf{V}[G\restriction A^{\prime}]
. Let
\beta\in B\setminus A^{\prime}
and let
f=\bigcap_{p\in G}p(\beta)
be the generic
\kappa
-real added by the
\beta
-th term of the product
\overline{\mathbb{S}}
.

Using the method of
5.17
on
\mathbb{S}_{\kappa}^{b,h_{\beta}}
we can show that
f\mathrel{\cancel{\in^{*}}}\varphi_{\xi}
for each
\xi\in\mu
.
∎

Theorem 5.20

(cf.
[
vdV23
]
Theorem 2.9)

If there exists
F\in\prod b
such that
2^{F}\leq b
and
2^{F(\alpha)}\leq F(\beta)
and
F(\alpha)=F(\alpha)^{|\alpha|}
for all
\alpha<\beta\in\kappa
, then there exist functions
h_{\xi}\in\prod b
for each
\xi<\kappa
such that for any cardinals
\lambda_{\xi}>\kappa
it is consistent that simultaneously
\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b,h_{\xi}}(\in^{*})=\lambda_{\xi}
for all
\xi<\kappa
.

Let
\left\langle S_{\xi}\mid\xi\in\kappa\right\rangle
be a disjoint family of stationary sets. We will assume without loss of generality that
\left\langle\lambda_{\xi}\mid\xi\in\kappa\right\rangle
is an increasing sequence. We define the following functions:

\displaystyle h_{\xi}(\alpha)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}(F(\alpha))^{+}&\text{ if }\alpha\in S_{\xi},\\
(2^{F(\alpha)})^{+}&\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

\displaystyle H_{\xi}(\alpha)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}F(\alpha)&\text{ if }\alpha\in\bigcup_{\eta\in\xi}S_{\eta},\\
2^{F(\alpha)}&\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

By our assumptions on
F
, it is clear that both
h_{\xi}
and
H_{\xi}
are increasing.
Let
A=\bigcup_{\xi\in\kappa}A_{\xi}
be the union of disjoint sets of ordinals such that
|A_{\xi}|=\lambda_{\xi}
, and for each
\beta\in A_{\xi}
define
h_{\beta}^{\prime}=H_{\xi}
. We consider
\overline{\mathbb{S}}=\prod^{\leq\kappa}_{\beta\in A}\mathbb{S}_{\kappa}^{b,h^{\prime}_{\beta}}
.

Note that
{\mathbb{S}^{b,H_{\xi}}_{\kappa}}
has the
(b,h_{\eta})
-Sacks property for all
\eta<\xi
. On the other hand, if
\eta\geq\xi
, then
h_{\eta}(\alpha)\leq H_{\xi}(\alpha)
for all
\alpha\in S_{\eta}
. It follows from
5.18
and
5.19
that:

\displaystyle\mathbf{V}[G]\vDash\text{``}\,\lambda_{\xi}=|A_{\xi}|\leq\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b,h_{\xi}}(\in^{*})\leq(\mathrm{Loc}_{\kappa}^{b,h_{\xi}})^{\mathbf{V}[G\restriction\bigcup_{\eta\leq\xi}A_{\eta}]}=\kappa^{+}\cdot\textstyle\sup_{\eta\leq\xi}|A_{\eta}|=\lambda_{\xi}\,\text{''}.

We may even do better than this, if we replace our
\kappa
-sized disjoint family of stationary sets by an almost disjoint family of stationary sets. The existence of such a family of size
\kappa^{+}
follows from
\lozenge_{\kappa}
. With a preparatory forcing, we can show the existence of a family of functions
\left\langle h_{\xi}\mid\xi\in\kappa^{+}\right\rangle
and show that there is a forcing extension in which all cardinals
\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b,h_{\xi}}(\in^{*})
are mutually different for each
\xi\in\kappa^{+}
. We refer to the third section of
[
vdV23
]
for a detailed construction.

5.4. Separating Cardinals of the Form
\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b,h}(\mathrel{\cancel{\ni^{\infty}}})

The forcing notion we consider dn this section can be seen as a
{}^{\kappa}\kappa
-analogue of the forcing notion used by Klausner & Mejía in
[
KM21
]
, and we will generally give a reference to the analogous definitions and lemmas as given in that article. In comparing our results to
[
KM21
]
, we once more have to be careful of the discrepancy mentioned in
5.10
.

In
[
KM21
]
it is shown that there exists a model in which there are uncountably many mutually different cardinals of the form
\mathfrak{d}^{b,h}_{\omega}(\mathrel{\cancel{\ni^{\infty}}})
. In fact, with a little bit more work, Cardona, Klausner & Mejía showed in
[
CKM21
]
that there exists a model in which there are
2^{\aleph_{0}}
many mutually different cardinals of each of the forms
\mathfrak{d}^{b,h}_{\omega}(\mathrel{\cancel{\in^{*}}})
,
\mathfrak{d}^{b,h}_{\omega}(\mathrel{\cancel{\ni^{\infty}}})
,
\mathfrak{b}^{b,h}_{\omega}(\mathrel{\cancel{\in^{*}}})
and
\mathfrak{b}^{b,h}_{\omega}(\mathrel{\cancel{\ni^{\infty}}})
. We will not prove such a strong result, and so far have only managed to give a family of
\kappa
many functions
b_{\alpha},h_{\alpha}
such that for any finite
A\subseteq\kappa
it is consistent that
\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b_{\alpha},h_{\alpha}}(\mathrel{\cancel{\ni^{\infty}}})
are mutually distinct for all
\alpha\in A
.

We will increase a cardinal of the form
\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b,h}(\mathrel{\cancel{\ni^{\infty}}})
by generically adding a slalom
\varphi\in\mathrm{Loc}_{\kappa}^{b,h}
such that
f\in^{\infty}\varphi
for all
f\in\prod b
from the ground model. At the same time, we will make sure that the forcing notion we use preserves
\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b^{\prime},h^{\prime}}(\mathrel{\cancel{\ni^{\infty}}})
for some other parameters
b^{\prime},h^{\prime}
. As with our construction from the previous section, we will consider a forcing notion consisting of generalised perfect trees. In this case we use trees on
\mathrm{Loc}_{<\kappa}^{b,h}
instead of trees on
\prod_{<\kappa}b
, and our generic object will be a
(b,h)
-slalom.

One main difference between our forcing notion and the forcing notion described in
[
KM21
]
, is that we will not work with uniform trees. That is, the forcing notion of
[
KM21
]
is comparable to Silver forcing, and has partial functions as conditions. Our forcing notion is comparable to Sacks forcing. We made the choice to use a Sacks-like forcing notion to form a better comparison with the Sacks-like forcing notion from the previous section. Both Silver-like and Sacks-like forcing notions will have the same effect on
\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b,h}(\mathrel{\cancel{\ni^{\infty}}})
, thus this change is not relevant for the results we will prove.

We will first define a norm on subsets of
[b(\alpha)]^{<h(\alpha)}
.

Definition 5.21

Given
M\subseteq[b(\alpha)]^{<h(\alpha)}
let
\left\lVert M\right\rVert_{b,\alpha}
be the least cardinal
\lambda\in\kappa
for which there exists
y\in[b(\alpha)]^{\lambda}
such that for all
x\in M
we have
y\not\subseteq x
, i.e., the least size of a subset
y
of
b(\alpha)
such that no superset of
y
is contained in
M
.

Definition 5.22

(cf.
[
KM21
]
3.1)

We define a forcing notion
{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
where conditions
T\in{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
are trees
T\subseteq\mathrm{Loc}_{<\kappa}^{b,h}
such that

(i)

for any
u\in T
there exists
v\in T
such that
u\subseteq v
and
v
is splitting,

(ii)

if
u\in\mathrm{Split}_{\alpha}(T)
, then
\left\lVert\mathrm{suc}(u,T)\right\rVert_{b,\mathrm{dom}(u)}\geq|\alpha|
,

(iii)

for any
u\in T
, if
\left\lVert\mathrm{suc}(u,T)\right\rVert_{b,\mathrm{dom}(u)}<2
, then
u
is non-splitting

(iv)

if
C\subseteq T
is a chain of splitting nodes with
|C|<\kappa
, then
\bigcup C
is a splitting node in
T
.

ordered by
T\leq S
iff

\bullet

T\subseteq S
and

\bullet

for every
u\in T
, if
\mathrm{suc}(u,T)\neq\mathrm{suc}(u,S)
, then
\left\lVert\mathrm{suc}(u,T)\right\rVert_{b,\mathrm{dom}(u)}<\left\lVert\mathrm{suc}(u,S)\right\rVert_{b,\mathrm{dom}(u)}
.

Here
(iii)
is necessary to ensure that the intersection of all trees in a generic filter forms a branch. If we allow
\left\lVert p(\alpha)\right\rVert_{b,\alpha}=1
without
p(\alpha)
being a singleton, then we have no way of decreasing the norm of
p(\alpha)
any further.

For
A
a set of ordinals and
T\in{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
, we define a
collapse
of
T\in{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
on
A
as a condition
T^{\prime}\leq T
such that
u
is non-splitting in
T^{\prime}
for all
u\in\mathrm{Lev}_{\alpha}(T^{\prime})
with
\alpha\in A
and
\mathrm{suc}(u,T^{\prime})=\mathrm{suc}(u,T)
for all
u\in\mathrm{Lev}_{\alpha}(T^{\prime})
with
\alpha\in\kappa\setminus A
. It is clear from the definition of the forcing notion that such a collapse exists for any set
A
that is the complement of a club set.

It is clear that
{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
adds a generic element to
\mathrm{Loc}_{\kappa}^{b,h}
, in the sense that if
G\subseteq{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
is a generic filter over
\mathbf{V}
, then
\varphi_{G}=\bigcap G\in\mathrm{Loc}_{\kappa}^{b,h}
and
\mathbf{V}[G]=\mathbf{V}[\varphi_{G}]
.

Definition 5.23

(cf.
[
KM21
]
3.2)

Define
({\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}})^{*}\subseteq{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
as the set of all
T\in{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
such that for each
\alpha\in\kappa
there exists
s_{\alpha}(T)\in\kappa
such that
\mathrm{Split}_{\alpha}(T)=\mathrm{Lev}_{s_{\alpha}(T)}(T)
and
\left\lVert\mathrm{suc}(u,T)\right\rVert_{b,s_{\alpha}(T)}\geq|s_{\alpha}(T)|
for all
u\in\mathrm{Split}_{\alpha}(T)
.

Lemma 5.24

({\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}})^{*}
densely embeds into
{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
.

Let
T\in{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
. Given
\alpha=\alpha_{0}\in\kappa
, let

\displaystyle\alpha_{n+1}=\sup\left\{\mathrm{dom}(u)\ \middle|\ u\in\mathrm{Split}_{\alpha_{n}}(T)\right\}.

Note that
\left\langle\alpha_{n}\mid n\in\omega\right\rangle
is increasing. Let
C=\left\{\sup_{n\in\omega}\alpha_{n}\mid\alpha\in\kappa\right\}
, then
C
is easily seen to be club. Note that if
\xi\in C
and
u\in\mathrm{Split}_{\xi}(T)
, then
\mathrm{dom}(u)=\xi
. By
(ii)
of
5.22
we see that
|\mathrm{dom}(u)|\leq\left\lVert\mathrm{suc}(u,T)\right\rVert_{b,\mathrm{dom}(u)}
for all
u\in\mathrm{Split}_{\xi}(T)
with
\xi\in C
.

Finally, let
T^{\prime}\leq T
be a collapse of
T
on
\kappa\setminus C
, then
T^{\prime}\in({\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}})^{*}
.
∎

Lemma 5.25

{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
is
{<}\kappa
-closed and
{<}(2^{\kappa})^{+}
-c.c..

Let
\lambda<\kappa
and
\langle T_{\xi}\in{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}\mid\xi\in\lambda\rangle
be a descending sequence of conditions, then
\bigcap_{\xi\in\lambda}T_{\xi}
is a condition below all
T_{\xi}
. The key observation in proving
{<}\kappa
-closure is the following claim: if
u\in T=\bigcap_{\xi\in\lambda}T_{\xi}
, then there is
\eta\in\lambda
such that
\mathrm{suc}(u,T)=\mathrm{suc}(u,T_{\eta})
. Checking that
T
satisfies
5.22
is easy with this claim in mind.

Suppose that
u\in T
and let
\lambda_{\xi}=\left\lVert\mathrm{suc}(u,T_{\xi})\right\rVert_{b,\mathrm{dom}(u)}
, then the ordering on
{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
dictates that
\left\langle\lambda_{\xi}\mid\xi\in\lambda\right\rangle
is a descending sequence of cardinals, hence there is
\eta\in\lambda
such that
\lambda_{\xi}=\lambda_{\eta}
for all
\xi\in[\eta,\lambda)
. But then
\mathrm{suc}(u,T_{\xi})=\mathrm{suc}(u,T_{\eta})
for all
\xi\in[\eta,\lambda)
by the ordering on
{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
.

That
{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
is
{<}(2^{\kappa})^{+}
-c.c. follows quickly from
|{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}|=2^{\kappa}
.
∎

As a corollary to the above lemma, we see that
{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
preserves all cardinalities
{\leq}\kappa
and
{>}2^{\kappa}
. We will prove the preservation of
\kappa^{+}
later, after
5.30
, thus actually we see that all cardinalities are preserved if we assume
2^{\kappa}=\kappa^{+}
in the ground model.

Define an ordering
T\leq_{\alpha}S
iff
T\leq S
and
\mathrm{Split}_{\alpha}(T)=\mathrm{Split}_{\alpha}(S)
. It is easy to see that this is a fusion ordering.

Lemma 5.26

(cf.
[
KM21
]
3.5(b))

{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
is closed under fusion, that is, if
\left\langle T_{\alpha}\mid\alpha\in\kappa\right\rangle
is a sequence in
{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
such that
T_{\beta}\leq_{\alpha}T_{\alpha}
for any
\beta>\alpha
, then
T=\bigcap_{\alpha\in\kappa}T_{\alpha}\in{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
and
T\leq_{\alpha}T_{\alpha}
for all
\alpha\in\kappa
.

Note that
\mathrm{Split}_{\alpha}(T_{\beta})=\mathrm{Split}_{\alpha}(T)
for
\alpha<\beta\in\kappa
. Showing that
T\in{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
is easy.
∎In the above lemma, we will note that if
T_{\alpha}\in({\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}})^{*}
for all
\alpha
, then
T\in({\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}})^{*}
as well.

Lemma 5.27

(cf.
[
KM21
]
3.4)

If
\alpha\in\kappa
,
T\in{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
and
\mathcal{D}\subseteq{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
is open dense, then there exists
T^{\prime}\leq_{\alpha}T
such that for any
v\in\mathrm{Split}_{\alpha+1}(T^{\prime})
we have
(T^{\prime})_{v}\in\mathcal{D}
. Furthermore, if
T\in({\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}})^{*}
, then we can also find
T^{\prime}\in({\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}})^{*}
satisfying the above.

Enumerate
\mathrm{Split}_{\alpha+1}(T)
as
\left\langle v_{\xi}\mid\xi\in\mu\right\rangle
and choose some
T_{\xi}\leq(T)_{v_{\xi}}\cap\mathcal{D}
for each
\xi\in\mu
, then
T^{\prime}=\bigcup_{\xi\in\mu}T_{\xi}
satisfies the above. Note that by construction
\mathrm{Split}_{\alpha+1}(T)\subseteq T^{\prime}
. Finally note that if
T\in({\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}})^{*}
and we choose each
T_{\xi}\in({\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}})^{*}
, then
T^{\prime}\in({\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}})^{*}
holds as well.
∎

The following lemma shows that
{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
satisfies a generalisation of Baumgartner’s Axiom
\mathsf{A}
to the context of
{}^{\kappa}\kappa
.

Lemma 5.28

(cf.
[
KM21
]
3.5(c))

If
A\subseteq{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
is an antichain,
T\in{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
and
\alpha\in\kappa
, then there is a condition
T^{\prime}\leq_{\alpha}T
in
{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
such that
T^{\prime}
is compatible with less than
\kappa
elements of
A
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be the set of
S\in{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
such that
S\leq R
for some
R\in A
or such that
S
is incompatible with all elements of
A
, then
\mathcal{D}
is open dense. By
5.27
there is
T^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
with
T^{\prime}\leq_{\alpha}T
such that
(T^{\prime})_{v}\in\mathcal{D}
for all
v\in\mathrm{Split}_{\alpha+1}(T^{\prime})
. Note that
|\mathrm{Split}_{\alpha+1}(T^{\prime})|<\kappa
and that
R\in A
is compatible with
T^{\prime}
iff there exists
v\in\mathrm{Split}_{\alpha+1}(T^{\prime})
such that
(T^{\prime})_{v}\leq R
. It follows that less than
\kappa
many elements of
A
are compatible with
T^{\prime}
.
∎

We will also prove that
{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
has continuous reading of names. In fact, we will prove a different property that implies continuous reading, which is referred to as
early reading of names
in
[
KM21
]
. The preservation of
\kappa^{+}
is a straightforward consequence of this property.

Definition 5.29

(cf.
[
KM21
]
3.6)

Let
T\in{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
and
\dot{\tau}
be a
{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
-name such that
T\Vdash\text{``}\,\dot{\tau}:\kappa\to\mathbf{V}\,\text{''}
. Then we say that
T

reads
\dot{\tau}
early
if
(T)_{u}
decides
\dot{\tau}\restriction\alpha
for every
\alpha\in\kappa
and
u\in\mathrm{Lev}_{\alpha}(T)
.

Lemma 5.30

(cf.
[
KM21
]
3.7)

Let
T\in{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
and
\dot{\tau}
a
{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
-name such that
T\Vdash\text{``}\,\dot{\tau}:\kappa\to\mathbf{V}\,\text{''}
. Then there exists
T^{\prime}\leq T
with
T^{\prime}\in({\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}})^{*}
such that
T^{\prime}
reads
\dot{\tau}
early.

Let
\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}=\{T\in{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}\mid T\text{ decides }\dot{\tau}\restriction\alpha\}
and note that
\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}
is open dense for each
\alpha\in\kappa
. We will construct a fusion sequence.

Let
T_{0}\leq T
be such that
T_{0}\in({\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}})^{*}
. Given
T_{\alpha}
, use
5.27
to define
T_{\alpha+1}\in({\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}})^{*}
such that
T_{\alpha+1}\leq_{\alpha}T_{\alpha}
and for any
v\in\mathrm{Split}_{\alpha+1}(T_{\alpha+1})
we have
(T_{\alpha+1})_{v}\in\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}
. For limit
\gamma\in\kappa
we already constructed the descending chain of conditions
\left\langle T_{\xi}\mid\xi\in\gamma\right\rangle
, so we let
T_{\gamma}=\bigcap_{\xi<\gamma}T_{\xi}
.

Let
T_{\kappa}
be the fusion limit of
\left\langle T_{\alpha}\mid\alpha\in\kappa\right\rangle
, then by construction we see that if
v\in\mathrm{Split}_{\alpha+1}(T_{\kappa})
, then
(T_{\kappa})_{v}
decides
\dot{\tau}\restriction\alpha
.

Finally, note that
\left\{s_{\alpha}(T_{\kappa})\mid\alpha\in\kappa\right\}
is club, so
C=\left\{\alpha\in\kappa\mid\alpha=s_{\alpha}(T_{\kappa})\right\}
is club as well. We define
T^{\prime}
to be a collapse of
T_{\kappa}
on
\kappa\setminus C
. Let
u\in T^{\prime}
with
\mathrm{dom}(u)=\alpha\in C
, then for any
\beta<\alpha
, we see that
s_{\beta}(T_{\kappa})<s_{\alpha}(T_{\kappa})=\alpha
, and therefore
(T_{\kappa})_{u\restriction(s_{\beta}(T_{\kappa})+1)}
decides
\dot{\tau}\restriction\beta
. But this implies that
(T_{\kappa})_{u}
decides
\dot{\tau}\restriction\alpha
, and hence also
(T^{\prime})_{u}
decides
\dot{\tau}\restriction\alpha
. On the other hand, if
u\in T^{\prime}
with
\mathrm{dom}(u)=\beta\notin C
, let
\alpha\in C
be minimal with
\beta<\alpha
, then
s_{\beta}(T_{\kappa})<\alpha
. Let
v\in T^{\prime}
be such that
u\subseteq v
and
\mathrm{dom}(v)=s_{\beta}(T_{\kappa})+1
, then
(T_{\kappa})_{v}
decides
\dot{\tau}\restriction\beta
. But, since
\bigcup_{\beta\leq\xi<\alpha}\mathrm{Lev}_{\xi}(T^{\prime})
contains no splitting nodes, we see that
(T^{\prime})_{u}=(T^{\prime})_{v}
, hence
(T^{\prime})_{u}
decides
\dot{\tau}\restriction\beta
.
∎

Corollary 5.31

{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
preserves
\kappa^{+}
.

Let
\dot{\tau}
be a name and
T\in{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
be such that
T\Vdash\text{``}\,\dot{\tau}:\kappa\to\kappa^{+}\,\text{''}
and
T
reads
\dot{\tau}
early. For each
\alpha\in\kappa
there is a set
B_{\alpha}\subseteq\kappa^{+}
with
|B_{\alpha}|\leq|\mathrm{Lev}_{\alpha}(T)|<\kappa
such that
T\Vdash\text{``}\,\dot{\tau}(\alpha)\in B_{\alpha}\,\text{''}
, thus
T\Vdash\text{``}\,\dot{\tau}[\kappa]\subseteq\bigcup_{\alpha\in\kappa}B_{\alpha}\,\text{''}
. Therefore
T\Vdash\text{``}\,\dot{\tau}\text{ is not surjective}\,\text{''}
.
∎

We will now look at the effect that
{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
has on the cardinality of anti-avoidance numbers. We first note that the generic slalom added by
{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
does not antilocalise any
f\in\prod b
from the ground model. By adding many such slaloms we can increase
\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b,h}(\mathrel{\cancel{\ni^{\infty}}})
.

Lemma 5.32

(cf.
[
KM21
]
3.3)

Let
\varphi_{G}\in\mathrm{Loc}_{\kappa}^{b,h}
be
{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
-generic over
\mathbf{V}
, let
h^{\prime}\leq^{*}b^{\prime}\in{}^{\kappa}\kappa
be cofinal increasing cardinal functions and let
S\subseteq\kappa
be stationary such that
h(\alpha)\leq h^{\prime}(\alpha)\leq b^{\prime}(\alpha)\leq b(\alpha)
for all
\alpha\in S
. If
\varphi^{\prime}_{G}\in\mathrm{Loc}_{h^{\prime}}^{b^{\prime}}
satisfies
\varphi^{\prime}_{G}(\alpha)=\varphi_{G}(\alpha)\cap b^{\prime}(\alpha)
for all
\alpha\in S
, then for any
f\in(\prod b^{\prime})^{\mathbf{V}}
we have
f\in^{\infty}\varphi_{G}^{\prime}
.

Note that this holds specifically for
b=b^{\prime}
and
h=h^{\prime}
, in which case
\varphi_{G}^{\prime}=\varphi_{G}
.

Working in
\mathbf{V}
, fix some
f\in\prod b^{\prime}
and
T\in({\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}})^{*}
and
\alpha_{0}\in\kappa
. Since
C=\left\{s_{\xi}(T)\mid\xi\in\kappa\right\}
is a club set, we can choose
\alpha>\alpha_{0}
such that
\alpha\in S\cap C
. We see that
\left\lVert\mathrm{suc}(u,T)\right\rVert_{b,\alpha}>1
for any
u\in\mathrm{Lev}_{\alpha}(t)
, since
u
is splitting.
5.21
implies that there is some
v\in\mathrm{suc}(u,T)
such that
\left\{f(\alpha)\right\}\subseteq v(\alpha)
. Then clearly
(T)_{v}\Vdash\text{``}\,f(\alpha)\in\dot{\varphi}_{G}(\alpha)\,\text{''}
, and because
f(\alpha)\in b^{\prime}(\alpha)
it follows that
(T)_{v}\Vdash\text{``}\,f(\alpha)\in\dot{\varphi}_{G}^{\prime}(\alpha)\,\text{''}
. Since
\alpha_{0}
was arbitrary,
\mathbf{V}[G]\vDash\text{``}\,f\in^{\infty}\varphi_{G}^{\prime}\,\text{''}
.
∎

On the other hand, we can give a condition for parameters
b^{\prime},h^{\prime}
such that the cardinal
\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b^{\prime},h^{\prime}}(\mathrel{\cancel{\ni^{\infty}}})
is preserved by
{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
. We first prove a condition such that
{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
has the
(b^{\prime},h^{\prime})
-Sacks property (see
5.14
) and then we use
4.5
to give a similar property to preserve antilocalisation.

Lemma 5.33

(cf.
[
KM21
]
3.13)

Let
b,h,b^{\prime},h^{\prime}\in{}^{\kappa}\kappa
be increasing cofinal cardinal functions such that for almost all
\alpha\in\kappa
we have
\left|\prod_{\xi\leq\alpha}[b(\xi)]^{<h(\xi)}\right|<h^{\prime}(\alpha)\leq b^{\prime}(\alpha)
.
If
\dot{f}
is a
{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
-name and
T\in{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
is such that
T\Vdash\text{``}\,\dot{f}\in\prod b^{\prime}\,\text{''}
, then there is
\varphi\in\mathrm{Loc}_{\kappa}^{b^{\prime},h^{\prime}}
and
T^{\prime}\leq T
such that
T^{\prime}\Vdash\text{``}\,\dot{f}(\xi)\in\varphi(\xi)\,\text{''}
for all
\xi<\kappa
.

Using
5.30
, let
T^{\prime}\in({\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}})^{*}
be such that
T^{\prime}\leq T
and
T^{\prime}
reads
\dot{f}
early, then we can define a unique
y_{v}\in b^{\prime}(\alpha)
for each
v\in\mathrm{Lev}_{\alpha+1}(T^{\prime})
with
(T^{\prime})_{v}\Vdash\text{``}\,\dot{f}(\alpha)=y_{v}\,\text{''}
. Note that
|\mathrm{Lev}_{\alpha+1}(T^{\prime})|\leq\left|\prod_{\xi\leq\alpha}[b(\xi)]^{<h(\xi)}\right|<h^{\prime}(\alpha)
. Therefore if
\varphi:\alpha\mapsto\left\{y_{v}\mid v\in\mathrm{Lev}_{\alpha+1}(T^{\prime})\right\}
, then
\varphi\in\mathrm{Loc}_{\kappa}^{b^{\prime},h^{\prime}}
and by construction we see that
T^{\prime}\Vdash\text{``}\,\dot{f}\in^{*}\varphi\,\text{''}
.
∎

Lemma 5.34

(cf.
[
KM21
]
3.15)

Let
b,h,b^{\prime},h^{\prime},\tilde{b},\tilde{h}\in{}^{\kappa}\kappa
be increasing cofinal cardinal functions such that for almost all
\alpha\in\kappa

\bullet

\left|\prod_{\xi\leq\alpha}[b(\xi)]^{<h(\xi)}\right|<h^{\prime}(\alpha)\leq\tilde{b}(\alpha)^{<\tilde{h}(\alpha)}\leq b^{\prime}(\alpha)
, and

\bullet

\tilde{h}(\alpha)\cdot h^{\prime}(\alpha)<\tilde{b}(\alpha)
.

If
\dot{\psi}
is a
{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
-name and
T\in{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
is such that
T\Vdash\text{``}\,\dot{\psi}\in\mathrm{Loc}_{\kappa}^{\tilde{b},\tilde{h}}\,\text{''}
, then there is
g\in\prod\tilde{b}
and
T^{\prime}\leq T
such that
T^{\prime}\Vdash\text{``}\,g\mathrel{\cancel{\in^{\infty}}}\dot{\psi}\,\text{''}
.

Working in the extension, we define the functions
\rho_{-},\rho_{+}
witnessing that
\mathscr{A}\!\!\mathscr{L}_{\tilde{b},\tilde{h}}\preceq\mathscr{L}_{b^{\prime},h^{\prime}}
and the injections
\iota_{\alpha}
for
\alpha\in\kappa
as in the proof of
4.5
. Note that if we assume that
\iota_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{V}
for each
\alpha
, then due to the constructive definition of
\rho_{-}
and
\rho_{+}
, it follows that
\rho_{-}\restriction(\mathrm{Loc}_{\kappa}^{\tilde{b},\tilde{h}})^{\mathbf{V}}
and
\rho_{+}\restriction(\mathrm{Loc}_{\kappa}^{b^{\prime},h^{\prime}})^{\mathbf{V}}
are in the ground model.

Let
\dot{f}
be a name for
\rho_{-}(\dot{\psi})
and by
5.33
, let
\varphi\in\mathrm{Loc}_{\kappa}^{b^{\prime},h^{\prime}}
and
T^{\prime}\leq T
be such that
T^{\prime}\Vdash\text{``}\,\dot{f}\in^{*}\varphi\,\text{''}
. Let
g=\varphi_{+}(\varphi)\in\prod\tilde{b}
, then
4.5
shows that
T^{\prime}\Vdash\text{``}\,g\mathrel{\cancel{\in^{\infty}}}\dot{\psi}\,\text{''}
.
∎

If we wish to increase
\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b,h}(\mathrel{\cancel{\ni^{\infty}}})
, we will have to add many new
\kappa
-reals. We will do so using a
{\leq}\kappa
-support product of forcing notions of the form
{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
. We could use a
{\leq}\kappa
-support iteration as well, but this has the drawback that we cannot increase
2^{\kappa}
past
\kappa^{++}
. We will show that the product behaves nicely, especially that the property of
5.34
is preserved under products, and that adding many
{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
-generic elements will indeed increase the size of
\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b,h}(\mathrel{\cancel{\ni^{\infty}}})
.

For the remainder of this section, we will fix some set of ordinals
\mathcal{A}
and
b_{\zeta},h_{\zeta}\in{}^{\kappa}\kappa
for each
\zeta\in\mathcal{A}
and we fix the abbreviations
\mathbb{Q}_{\zeta}={\mathbb{Q}^{b_{\zeta},h_{\zeta}}_{\kappa}}
and
\mathbb{Q}_{\zeta}^{*}=({\mathbb{Q}^{b_{\zeta},h_{\zeta}}_{\kappa}})^{*}
. We define the
{\leq}\kappa
-support products
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}=\prod^{\leq\kappa}_{\zeta\in\mathcal{A}}\mathbb{Q}_{\zeta}
and
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{*}=\prod^{\leq\kappa}_{\zeta\in\mathcal{A}}\mathbb{Q}_{\zeta}^{*}
. Since each
\mathbb{Q}^{*}_{\zeta}
densely embeds in
\mathbb{Q}_{\zeta}
, it is easy to see that
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{*}
densely embeds in
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
. We will often implicitly assume without mention that all conditions are in
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{*}
.

Lemma 5.35

If
\kappa^{+}=2^{\kappa}
, then
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
has the
{<}\kappa^{++}
-cc.

Since each
\mathbb{Q}_{\zeta}
has the
{<}\kappa^{++}
-cc, we can use a standard application of the
\Delta
-system lemma to prove that
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
has the
{<}\kappa^{++}
-cc as well.
∎

Lemma 5.36

\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
is
{<}\kappa
-closed.

If
\left\langle p_{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\gamma\right\rangle
is a sequence of conditions in
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
such that
p_{\alpha^{\prime}}\leq_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}p_{\alpha}
for all
\alpha\leq\alpha^{\prime}
, then we define
\bigwedge\!_{\alpha<\gamma}p_{\alpha}:{\mathcal{A}}\to\bigcup_{\zeta\in{\mathcal{A}}}\mathbb{Q}_{\zeta}
to be the function
\zeta\mapsto\bigcap_{\alpha\in\gamma}p_{\alpha}(\zeta)
, which is a condition in
\mathbb{Q}_{\zeta}
by
5.25
. If we assume that
\gamma<\kappa
, then it is easy to see that
|\mathrm{supp}(\bigwedge\!_{\alpha<\gamma}p_{\alpha})|\leq\kappa
as well, so
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
is
{<}\kappa
-closed.
∎We will be using the notation
\bigwedge\!_{\alpha<\gamma}p_{\alpha}
from the above proof in the rest of this section as well.

Lemma 5.37

(cf.
[
KM21
]
5.5)

\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{*}
is closed under generalised fusion, that is, for any generalised fusion sequence
\left\langle(p_{\alpha},Z_{\alpha})\mid\alpha\in\kappa\right\rangle
there exists
p\leq_{\alpha,Z_{\alpha}}p_{\alpha}
with
\mathrm{supp}(p)=\bigcup_{\alpha\in\kappa}\mathrm{supp}(p_{\alpha})
.

Let
S=\bigcup_{\alpha\in\kappa}\mathrm{supp}(p_{\alpha})
, then
\bigcup_{\alpha\in\kappa}Z_{\alpha}=S
, so for each
\zeta\in S
we can fix
\alpha_{\zeta}\in\kappa
such that
\zeta\in Z_{\alpha_{\zeta}}
. Then also
\zeta\in Z_{\alpha}
for any
\alpha\geq\alpha_{\zeta}
, since
Z_{\alpha}\supseteq Z_{\alpha_{\zeta}}
. If
\alpha_{\zeta}\leq\alpha\leq\beta<\kappa
, then
p_{\beta}\leq_{\alpha,Z_{\alpha}}p_{\alpha}
, and thus by
\zeta\in Z_{\alpha}
we see that
p_{\beta}(\zeta)\leq_{\alpha}p_{\alpha}(\zeta)
. Therefore
\left\langle p_{\alpha_{\zeta}+\alpha}(\zeta)\mid\alpha\in\kappa\right\rangle
is a fusion sequence in
\mathbb{Q}_{\zeta}^{*}
and we can define
p(\zeta)=\bigcap_{\alpha\in\kappa}p_{\alpha_{\zeta}+\alpha}(\zeta)
, then
p(\zeta)\in\mathbb{Q}_{\zeta}^{*}
by
5.26
.
∎

For
p\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
and
\alpha\in\kappa
, we define the set of
possibilities

\mathrm{poss}(p,{<}\alpha)
to be the set of functions
\eta
with domain
\mathrm{supp}(p)
such that
\eta(\zeta)\in\mathrm{Lev}_{\alpha}(p(\zeta))
for all
\zeta\in\mathrm{supp}(p)
. We similarly define
\mathrm{poss}(p,{\leq}\alpha)=\mathrm{poss}(p,{<}\alpha+1)
. Remember that
(p(\zeta))_{u}
is the subtree of
p(\zeta)
generated by
u
. If
\eta\in\mathrm{poss}(p,{<}\alpha)
, we define
\eta\land p
to be the condition with
(\eta\land p)(\zeta)=(p(\zeta))_{\eta(\zeta)}
for
\zeta\in\mathrm{supp}(p)
and
(\eta\land p)(\zeta)=\mathbbm{1}_{\zeta}
otherwise. We sometimes abuse this notation also to define
\eta\land q
for
q\leq p
with larger support, where we let
(\eta\land q)(\zeta)=q(\zeta)
for all
\zeta\in\mathrm{supp}(q)\setminus\mathrm{supp}(p)
.

For
p\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{*}
, we define
\mathrm{Split}(p)=\bigcup_{\zeta\in\mathrm{supp}(p)}\left\{s_{\alpha}(p(\zeta))\mid\alpha\in\kappa\right\}
and let
\left\langle\overline{s}_{\alpha}(p)\mid\alpha\in\kappa\right\rangle
be the strictly increasing enumeration of
\mathrm{Split}(p)
. Let
\mathcal{Z}^{p}(\alpha)=\left\{\zeta\in\mathrm{supp}(p)\mid\exists\xi(s_{\xi}(p(\zeta))=\alpha)\right\}
.

We call
p\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{*}

modest
if for any
\alpha\in\kappa
we have
|\mathrm{poss}(p,{<}\alpha)|<\kappa
and
|\mathcal{Z}^{p}(\alpha)|\leq\alpha
, and moreover
|\mathcal{Z}^{p}(\overline{s}_{\alpha}(p))|=1
in case
\alpha
is successor.

Lemma 5.38

The set of modest conditions is dense in
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{*}
(hence in
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
as well).

Let
p\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{*}
. We will assume for convenience (and without loss of generality) that
|\mathrm{supp}(p)|=\kappa
. Enumerate
\mathrm{supp}(p)
as
\left\langle\zeta_{\alpha}\mid\alpha\in\kappa\right\rangle
and let
v_{\alpha}\in\mathrm{Lev}_{\alpha+1}(p(\zeta_{\alpha}))
be arbitrary. We define
q
as

\displaystyle q(\zeta_{\alpha})

\displaystyle=(p(\zeta_{\alpha}))_{v_{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\text{ for all }\alpha\in\kappa

\displaystyle q(\zeta)

\displaystyle=\mathbbm{1}_{\zeta}

\displaystyle\text{ if }\zeta\notin\mathrm{supp}(p)

Then
q\leq p
and
|\mathrm{poss}(q,{<}\alpha)|<\kappa
and
|\mathcal{Z}^{q}(\alpha)|\leq\alpha
for all
\alpha\in\kappa
. Note that if
r\leq q
is such that
\mathrm{supp}(r)=\mathrm{supp}(q)
, then
|\mathrm{poss}(r,{<}\alpha)|<\kappa
and
|\mathcal{Z}^{r}(\alpha)|\leq\alpha
still hold for all
\alpha\in\kappa
.

We will define
r\leq q
such that
r
is modest. Note that
C=\left\{\overline{s}_{\alpha}(q)\mid\alpha\in\kappa\text{ is limit}\right\}
contains a club set. Let
A_{\zeta}=\left\{\overline{s}_{\alpha}(q)\mid\alpha\in\kappa\text{ is successor}\land\zeta\neq\min(\mathcal{Z}^{q}(\overline{s}_{\alpha}(q)))\right\}
. For any
\zeta\in\mathrm{supp}(q)
, we define
r(\zeta)
to be a collapse of
q(\zeta)
on
A_{\zeta}
, and for any
\zeta\notin\mathrm{supp}(q)
we let
r(\zeta)=\mathbbm{1}_{\zeta}
. It is clear from this construction that
\mathrm{Split}(r)=\mathrm{Split}(q)
, and that
\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}^{r}(\overline{s}_{\alpha}(r))
implies that
\alpha
is limit or that
\zeta=\min(\mathcal{Z}^{q}(\overline{s}_{\alpha}(q)))
. In other words,
r
is modest.

Finally
r(\zeta)\in\mathbb{Q}_{\zeta}^{*}
is implied by
p(\zeta)\in\mathbb{Q}_{\zeta}^{*}
, because for each
\alpha\in\kappa
there is
\beta\geq\alpha
such that
s_{\alpha}(r(\zeta))=s_{\beta}(p(\zeta))
, and for any
\alpha\in\mathrm{Split}(r)
and
\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}^{r}(\alpha)
we have
\mathrm{suc}(u,r(\zeta))=\mathrm{suc}(u,p(\zeta))
for all
u\in\mathrm{Lev}_{\alpha}(r)
. Notably, for the norm we see that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathrm{suc}(u,r(\zeta))\right\rVert_{b,\mathrm{dom}(u)}=\left\lVert\mathrm{suc}(u,p(\zeta))\right\rVert_{b,\mathrm{dom}(u)}\geq\beta\geq\alpha.

The reason we are interested in modest conditions, is that modest conditions behave similarly to conditions of the single forcing notion
{\mathbb{Q}^{b,h}_{\kappa}}
. By using modest conditions, we get a bound below
\kappa
to the number of possibilities up to a certain height
\alpha
, which will be crucial in preservation of the Sacks property (
5.43
).
We will first use modest conditions to generalise
5.27
to products.

In order to state the lemma, we define one more ordering on
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{*}
as follows:
q\leq_{\alpha}^{*}p
if

\bullet

q\leq p
and

\bullet

\mathrm{Lev}_{\overline{s}_{\alpha}(p)}(q(\zeta))=\mathrm{Lev}_{\overline{s}_{\alpha}(p)}(p(\zeta))
for all
\zeta\in\mathrm{supp}(p)
and

\bullet

s_{0}(q(\zeta))>\overline{s}_{\alpha}(p)
for any
\zeta\in\mathrm{supp}(q)\setminus\mathrm{supp}(p)
.

Lemma 5.39

(cf.
[
KM21
]
5.4)

If
\alpha\in\kappa
,
p\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{*}
is modest and
\mathcal{D}\subseteq\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
is open dense, then there exists
q\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{*}
with
q\leq_{\alpha}^{*}p
such that for any
\eta\in\mathrm{poss}(q,{\leq}\overline{s}_{\alpha}(q))
we have
\eta\land q\in\mathcal{D}
.

By assumption
p
is modest, thus if
\left\langle\eta_{\xi}\mid\xi\in\mu\right\rangle
enumerates
\mathrm{poss}(p,{\leq}\overline{s}_{\alpha}(p))
, then
\mu<\kappa
. We create a descending sequence of conditions
\left\langle p_{\xi}\mid\xi\in\mu\right\rangle
. Let
p_{0}=p
. If
\gamma<\mu
is limit, let
p_{\gamma}=\bigwedge\!_{\xi<\gamma}p_{\xi}
using
5.36
. If
p_{\xi}
has been defined, let
p_{\xi+1}^{\prime}\leq\eta_{\xi}\land p_{\xi}
be such that
p_{\xi+1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{D}
. We define
p_{\xi+1}
from
p_{\xi+1}^{\prime}
in a pointwise manner. If
\zeta\in\mathrm{supp}(p)
, we let

\displaystyle p_{\xi+1}(\zeta)=p_{\xi+1}^{\prime}(\zeta)\cup\textstyle\bigcup\left\{(p_{\xi}(\zeta))_{v}\mid v\in\mathrm{Lev}_{\overline{s}_{\alpha}(p)+1}(p_{\xi}(\zeta))\text{ and }v\neq\eta_{\xi}(\zeta)\right\}.

In plain words, we keep
p_{\xi+1}(\zeta)
almost equal to
p_{\xi}(\zeta)
, except that we replace the part of
p_{\xi}(\zeta)
that extends
\eta_{\xi}(\zeta)
with the tree
p_{\xi+1}^{\prime}(\zeta)
. On the other hand, if
\zeta\in\mathrm{supp}(p_{\xi+1}^{\prime})\setminus\mathrm{supp}(p)
, we let
p_{\xi+1}(\zeta)=(p_{\xi+1}^{\prime}(\zeta))_{u}
for some
u\in\mathrm{Lev}_{\overline{s}_{\alpha}(p)+1}(p_{\xi+1}^{\prime}(\zeta))
. Finally if
\zeta\notin\mathrm{supp}(p^{\prime}_{\xi+1})
we let
p_{\xi+1}(\zeta)=\mathbbm{1}_{\zeta}
.

Note that
p_{\xi+1}^{\prime}(\zeta)=(p_{\xi+1}^{\prime}(\zeta))_{\eta_{\xi}(\zeta)}=(p_{\xi+1}(\zeta))_{\eta_{\xi}(\zeta)}
for all
\zeta\in\mathrm{supp}(p)
, and thus
\eta_{\xi}\land p_{\xi+1}=p_{\xi+1}^{\prime}
.

Finally define
q=\bigwedge\!_{\xi\in\mu}p_{\xi}
, then we see that
q\leq_{\alpha}^{*}p
and
\eta\land q\leq\eta\land p_{\xi+1}=p_{\xi+1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{D}
for each
\eta\in\mathrm{poss}(q,{\leq}\overline{s}_{\alpha}(q))=\mathrm{poss}(p,{\leq}\overline{s}_{\alpha}(p))
.
∎

We can also generalise the notion of early reading in the most apparent sense.

Definition 5.40

Let
p\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
and
\dot{\tau}
be a
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
-name such that
p\Vdash\text{``}\,\dot{\tau}:\kappa\to\mathbf{V}\,\text{''}
, then we say that
p
reads
\dot{\tau}

early
iff
\eta\land p
decides
\dot{\tau}\restriction\alpha
for every
\alpha\in\kappa
and
\eta\in\mathrm{poss}(p,{<}\alpha)
.

Lemma 5.41

(cf.
[
KM21
]
5.6)

Let
p\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
and
\dot{\tau}
a
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
-name such that
p\Vdash\text{``}\,\dot{\tau}:\kappa\to\mathbf{V}\,\text{''}
. Then there exists
q\leq p
with
q\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{*}
such that
q
reads
\dot{\tau}
early.

Let
\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}=\left\{q\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\mid q\text{ decides }\dot{\tau}\restriction\alpha\right\}
, and note that
\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}
is open dense for each
\alpha\in\kappa
. We prove the lemma by constructing
q^{\prime}\leq p
such that
\eta\land q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}
for all
\eta\in\mathrm{poss}(q^{\prime},{\leq}\alpha)
. We claim that this is sufficient: define
q
such that
q(\zeta)
is a collapse of
q^{\prime}(\zeta)
on
\left\{\overline{s}_{\alpha}(q^{\prime})\mid\alpha\text{ is successor}\right\}
for each
\zeta\in\mathrm{supp}(q^{\prime})
and
q(\zeta)=\mathbbm{1}_{\zeta}
otherwise, then
q
reads
\dot{\tau}
early.

The condition
q^{\prime}
will be the limit of a generalised fusion sequence
\left\langle(p_{\alpha},Z_{\alpha})\mid\alpha\in\kappa\right\rangle
. Each
p_{\alpha}
will be modest and have the following property:

\displaystyle(\star_{\alpha})\qquad s_{\alpha}(p_{\alpha}(\zeta))<s_{0}(p_{\alpha}(\zeta^{\prime})\text{ for all }\zeta\in Z_{\alpha}\text{ and }\zeta^{\prime}\in\mathrm{supp}(p_{\alpha})\setminus Z_{\alpha}

Given
p_{\alpha}
satisfying
(\star_{\alpha})
, let
\beta_{\alpha}=\sup\left\{s_{\alpha}(p_{\alpha}(\zeta))\mid\zeta\in Z_{\alpha}\right\}
and suppose
p_{\alpha+1}\leq_{\beta_{\alpha}}^{*}p_{\alpha}
, then it follows from
(\star_{\alpha})
that
p_{\alpha+1}\leq_{\alpha,Z_{\alpha}}p_{\alpha}
.

Firstly, we let
p_{0}\leq p
be modest such that
p_{0}\in\mathcal{D}_{\overline{s}_{0}(p_{0})}
. This can be easily achieved by letting
p_{0}^{0}\leq p
be modest, finding modest
p_{0}^{n+1}\leq p_{0}^{n}
such that
p_{0}^{n+1}\in\mathcal{D}_{\overline{s}_{0}(p_{0}^{n})}
and letting
p_{0}=\bigwedge\!_{n\in\omega}p_{0}^{n}
. We set
Z_{0}=\mathcal{Z}^{p_{0}}(\overline{s}_{0}(p_{0}))
, then
|Z_{0}|<\kappa
by modesty and
p_{0}
satisfies
(\star_{0})
. We may also assume that
|Z_{0}|
is infinite and that
|\mathrm{supp}(p_{0})|=\kappa
.

Next, for limit
\gamma
, we have
Z_{\gamma}=\bigcup_{\alpha\in\gamma}Z_{\alpha}
and
\hat{p}_{\gamma}=\bigwedge\!_{\alpha<\gamma}p_{\gamma}
. We let
p_{\gamma}\leq_{\gamma,Z_{\gamma}}\hat{p}_{\gamma}
be such that it has
(\star_{\gamma})
. This is possible, since we may keep
p_{\gamma}(\zeta)=\hat{p}_{\gamma}(\zeta)
for all
\zeta\in Z_{\gamma}
and thus trivially have
p_{\gamma}\leq_{\gamma,Z_{\gamma}}\hat{p}_{\gamma}
. The construction of the successor step will show that
\beta_{\gamma}=s_{\gamma}(p_{\gamma}(\zeta))=s_{\gamma}(p_{\gamma}(\zeta^{\prime}))
for any
\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}\in Z_{\gamma}
. If
\eta\in\mathrm{poss}(p_{\gamma},{<}\beta_{\gamma})
and
\delta<\beta_{\gamma}
, then there is
\alpha<\gamma
such that
\eta\land p_{\alpha}\in\mathcal{D}_{\delta}
, therefore
\eta\land p_{\gamma}\in\mathcal{D}_{\beta_{\gamma}}
.

Finally we construct
p_{\alpha+1}
from
p_{\alpha}
. Let
\lambda=|Z_{\alpha}|
and enumerate
Z_{\alpha}
as
\left\langle\zeta_{\xi}\mid\xi<\lambda\right\rangle
. We use bookkeeping to fulfil the promise that
\bigcup_{\alpha\in\kappa}Z_{\alpha}=\bigcup_{\alpha\in\kappa}\mathrm{supp}(p_{\alpha})
, thereby setting
Z_{\alpha+1}=Z_{\alpha}\cup\left\{\zeta_{\lambda}\right\}
for some appropriate
\zeta_{\lambda}\in\mathrm{supp}(p_{\alpha})\setminus Z_{\alpha}
. We construct a descending sequence of conditions
\langle p_{\alpha}^{\xi}\mid\xi\leq\lambda+\alpha+1\rangle
by recursion over a strictly increasing sequence of ordinals
\langle\delta_{\alpha}^{\xi}\mid\xi\leq\lambda+\alpha+1\rangle
, to obtain the following properties:

(i)

p_{\alpha}^{0}\leq^{*}_{\beta_{\alpha}}p_{\alpha}
, where
\beta_{\alpha}=\sup\left\{s_{\alpha}(p_{\alpha}(\zeta))\mid\zeta\in Z_{\alpha}\right\}

(ii)

p_{\alpha}^{\xi^{\prime}}\leq_{\delta_{\alpha}^{\xi}}^{*}p_{\alpha}^{\xi}
for all
\xi<\xi^{\prime}\leq\lambda+\alpha+1

(iii)

\delta_{\alpha}^{\xi}=s_{\alpha+1}(p_{\alpha}^{\xi}(\zeta_{\xi}))
for all
\xi<\lambda

(iv)

\delta_{\alpha}^{\xi}<s_{\alpha+1}(p_{\alpha}^{\xi}(\zeta_{\xi^{\prime}}))
for all
\xi<\xi^{\prime}<\lambda

(v)

\delta_{\alpha}^{\xi}<s_{0}(p_{\alpha}^{\xi}(\zeta))
for all
\xi<\lambda
and
\zeta\in\mathrm{supp}(p_{\alpha}^{\xi})\setminus Z_{\alpha}

(vi)

\delta_{\alpha}^{\lambda+\varepsilon}=s_{\varepsilon}(p_{\alpha}^{\lambda+\varepsilon}(\zeta_{\lambda}))
for all
\varepsilon<\alpha

(vii)

\delta_{\alpha}^{\lambda+\varepsilon}<s_{\alpha+2}(p_{\alpha}^{\lambda+\varepsilon}(\zeta_{\xi}))
for all
\varepsilon<\alpha
and
\xi<\lambda

(viii)

\delta_{\alpha}^{\lambda+\varepsilon}<s_{0}(p_{\alpha}^{\lambda+\varepsilon}(\zeta))
for all
\varepsilon<\alpha
and
\zeta\in\mathrm{supp}(p_{\alpha}^{\lambda+\varepsilon})\setminus Z_{\alpha+1}

(ix)

For all
\xi\leq\lambda+\alpha+1
and any
\eta\in\mathrm{poss}(p_{\alpha}^{\xi},{\leq}\delta_{\alpha}^{\xi})
we have
\eta\land p_{\alpha}^{\xi}\in\mathcal{D}_{\delta_{\alpha}^{\xi}}
.

We will set
p_{\alpha+1}=p_{\alpha}^{\lambda+\alpha+1}
. By construction
p_{\alpha+1}
satisfies
(\star_{\alpha+1})
and
p_{\alpha+1}\leq_{\alpha,Z_{\alpha}}p_{\alpha}
.

s_{\alpha^{\prime}}(p_{\alpha^{\prime}}(\zeta_{0}))
s_{\alpha^{\prime}}(p_{\alpha^{\prime}}(\zeta_{1}))
s_{\alpha^{\prime}}(p_{\alpha^{\prime}}(\zeta_{2}))
s_{\alpha^{\prime}}(p_{\alpha^{\prime}}(\zeta_{3}))
s_{\alpha^{\prime}}(p_{\alpha^{\prime}}(\zeta_{\xi}))
s_{0}(p_{\alpha^{\prime}}(\zeta_{\lambda}))
s_{1}(p_{\alpha^{\prime}}(\zeta_{\lambda}))
s_{2}(p_{\alpha^{\prime}}(\zeta_{\lambda}))
s_{3}(p_{\alpha^{\prime}}(\zeta_{\lambda}))
s_{\alpha^{\prime}}(p_{\alpha^{\prime}}(\zeta_{\lambda}))
s_{0}(p_{\alpha^{\prime}}(\zeta))
\beta_{\alpha}
\delta_{\alpha}^{0}
\delta_{\alpha}^{1}
\delta_{\alpha}^{2}
\delta_{\alpha}^{3}
\delta_{\alpha}^{\xi}
\delta_{\alpha}^{\lambda}
\delta_{\alpha}^{\lambda+1}
\delta_{\alpha}^{\lambda+2}
\delta_{\alpha}^{\lambda+3}
\delta_{\alpha}^{\lambda+\alpha^{\prime}}
\zeta_{0}
\zeta_{1}
\zeta_{2}
\zeta_{3}
\zeta_{\xi}
(
\xi<\lambda
)
\zeta_{\lambda}
\zeta
(
\zeta\notin Z_{\alpha^{\prime}}
)

Figure 5:
The structure of
\mathrm{Split}(p_{\alpha+1})
. We write
\alpha+1
as
\alpha^{\prime}
for brevity.

The result of this construction is summarised in
Figure

5
. Let us clarify this diagram and the recursive construction construction. The initial splitting levels
s_{\alpha}(p_{\alpha}(\zeta_{\xi}))
with
\xi<\lambda
occur below
\beta_{\alpha}
and are left unmodified during the entire construction. The ordinals
\delta_{\alpha}^{\xi}
give us the height of
s_{\alpha+1}(p_{\alpha+1}(\zeta_{\xi}))
with
\xi<\lambda
, and the ordinals
\delta_{\alpha}^{\lambda+\varepsilon}
give the height of
s_{\varepsilon}(p_{\alpha+1}(\zeta_{\lambda}))
. For any other
\zeta\in\mathrm{supp}(p_{\alpha+1})\setminus Z_{\alpha+1}
the splitting starts strictly above
\beta_{\alpha+1}
, that is,
s_{0}(p_{\alpha+1}(\zeta))>s_{\alpha+1}(p_{\alpha+1}(\zeta_{\lambda}))
. At step
\xi
of the recursive construction, we decide on
\delta_{\alpha}^{\xi}
and hence on the splitting levels up to
\delta_{\alpha}^{\xi}
, making sure that the splitting levels we have not considered yet occur at a strictly higher level. We use
5.39
to make sure we satisfy
(ix)
without disturbing the splitting levels up to
\delta_{\alpha}^{\xi}
. This automatically gives us modesty as well.
∎

We are now ready to give the last two lemmas that give us the effect of
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
on
\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b,h}(\mathrel{\cancel{\ni^{\infty}}})
.
5.42
is a generalisation of
5.32
and shows that we can increase
\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b,h}(\mathrel{\cancel{\ni^{\infty}}})
with
\mathbb{\overline{Q}}
, and
5.43
gives us the preservation of the Sacks property, which is a generalisation of
5.33
.

Lemma 5.42

Let
\mathcal{B}\subseteq\mathcal{A}
be sets of ordinals, with
\mathcal{B}^{c}=\mathcal{A}\setminus\mathcal{B}
, and let
\left\langle h_{\zeta},b_{\zeta}\mid\zeta\in\mathcal{A}\right\rangle
be a sequence of cofinal increasing cardinal functions such that
h_{\zeta}\leq^{*}b_{\zeta}
for all
\zeta\in\mathcal{A}
. Let
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}=\prod_{\zeta\in\mathcal{A}}^{\leq\kappa}\mathbb{Q}_{\zeta}
, let
G
be
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
-generic over
\mathbf{V}
and
\mathbf{V}\vDash\text{``}\,2^{\kappa}=\kappa^{+}\,\text{''}
. If
h^{\prime}\leq^{*}b^{\prime}\in{}^{\kappa}\kappa
are cofinal increasing cardinal functions such that for each
\zeta\in\mathcal{B}
there exists a stationary set
S_{\zeta}\subseteq\kappa
such that
h_{\zeta}(\alpha)\leq h^{\prime}(\alpha)\leq b^{\prime}(\alpha)\leq b_{\zeta}(\alpha)
for all
\alpha\in S_{\zeta}
. Then
\mathbf{V}[G]\vDash\text{``}\,|\mathcal{B}|\leq\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b^{\prime},h^{\prime}}(\mathrel{\cancel{\ni^{\infty}}})\,\text{''}
.

The lemma is trivial if
|\mathcal{B}|\leq\kappa^{+}
, thus we assume
\kappa^{++}\leq|\mathcal{B}|
.

We work in
\mathbf{V}[G]
. Let
\mu<|\mathcal{B}|
and let
\left\{f_{\xi}\mid\xi<\mu\right\}\subseteq\prod b^{\prime}
, then we want to describe some
\varphi\in\mathrm{Loc}_{\kappa}^{b^{\prime},h^{\prime}}
such that
f_{\xi}\in^{\infty}\varphi
for each
\xi<\mu
. Since
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
is
{<}\kappa^{++}
-cc, we could find
A_{\xi}\subseteq\mathcal{A}
with
|A_{\xi}|\leq\kappa^{+}
for each
\xi<\mu
such that
f_{\xi}\in\mathbf{V}[G\restriction A_{\xi}]
. Since
|\mathcal{B}|>\mu\cdot\kappa^{+}
, we may fix some
\beta\in\mathcal{B}\setminus\bigcup_{\xi<\mu}A_{\xi}
for the remainder of this proof. Let
\varphi_{\beta}=\bigcap_{p\in G}p(\beta)
be the
\mathbb{Q}_{\beta}
-generic
\kappa
-real added by the
\beta
-th term of the product
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
, and let
\varphi^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Loc}_{\kappa}^{b^{\prime},h^{\prime}}
be such that
\varphi^{\prime}(\alpha)=\varphi_{\beta}(\alpha)\cap b^{\prime}(\alpha)
for each
\alpha\in S_{\beta}
.

Continuing the proof in the ground model, let
\dot{\varphi}^{\prime}
be a
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
-name for
\varphi^{\prime}
and
\dot{f}_{\xi}
be a
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
-name for
f_{\xi}
, let
p\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{*}
and
\alpha_{0}<\kappa
. We want to find some
\alpha\geq\alpha_{0}
and
q\leq p
such that
q\Vdash\text{``}\,\dot{f}_{\xi}(\alpha)\in\dot{\varphi}^{\prime}(\alpha)\,\text{''}
.

We now reason as in
5.32
. Let
C=\left\{s_{\xi}(p(\beta))\mid\xi\in\kappa\right\}
, then
C
is club. Therefore, there exists
\alpha\in S_{\beta}\cap C
with
\alpha\geq\alpha_{0}
. Choose some
p_{0}\leq p
such that
p_{0}(\beta)=p(\beta)
and such that there is a
\gamma\in b^{\prime}(\alpha)
for which
p_{0}\Vdash\text{``}\,\dot{f}_{\xi}(\alpha)=\gamma\,\text{''}
. This is possible, since
f_{\xi}\in\mathbf{V}[G\restriction A_{\xi}]
and
\beta\notin A_{\xi}
, therefore we could find
p_{0}^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\restriction A_{\xi}
with
p_{0}^{\prime}\leq p\restriction A_{\xi}
and
\gamma
with the aforementioned property, and then let
p_{0}(\eta)=p^{\prime}_{0}(\eta)
if
\eta\in A_{\xi}
and
p_{0}(\eta)=p(\eta)
otherwise.

Note that
\alpha\in C
implies that
\left\lVert\mathrm{suc}(u,p_{0}(\beta))\right\rVert_{b_{\beta},\alpha}=\left\lVert\mathrm{suc}(u,p(\beta))\right\rVert_{b_{\beta},\alpha}>1
for all
u\in\mathrm{Lev}_{\alpha}(p_{0}(\beta))
, and that
\alpha\in S_{\beta}
implies that
\gamma\in b^{\prime}(\alpha)\subseteq b_{\beta}(\alpha)
. Consequently, there exists
v\in\mathrm{suc}(u,p_{0}(\beta))
with
\gamma\in v(\alpha)
. Note that
v(\alpha)\in[b_{\beta}(\alpha)]^{<h_{\beta}(\alpha)}
, and
h_{\beta}(\alpha)\leq h^{\prime}(\alpha)
in virtue of
\alpha\in S_{\beta}
. It follows that
(p_{0}(\beta))_{v}\Vdash\text{``}\,\dot{\varphi}_{\beta}(\alpha)=v(\alpha)\,\text{''}
, where
\dot{\varphi}_{\beta}
names the generic
(b_{\beta},h_{\beta})
-slalom. Define
q\leq p_{0}
by
q(\zeta)=p_{0}(\zeta)
if
\zeta\in\mathcal{A}\setminus\left\{\beta\right\}
and
q(\beta)=(p_{0}(\beta))_{v}
, then we see that
q\Vdash\text{``}\,\gamma\in v(\alpha)=\dot{\varphi}_{\beta}(\alpha)\land\gamma\in b^{\prime}(\alpha)\,\text{''}
, and thus
q\Vdash\text{``}\,\dot{f}_{\xi}(\alpha)=\gamma\in\dot{\varphi}_{\beta}(\alpha)\cap b^{\prime}(\alpha)=\dot{\varphi}^{\prime}(\alpha)\,\text{''}

Since
\alpha_{0}
was arbitrary, it follows that
\mathbf{V}[G]\vDash\text{``}\,f_{\xi}\in^{\infty}\varphi^{\prime}\,\text{''}
for each
\xi<\mu
.
∎

Lemma 5.43

Let
\mathcal{B}\subseteq\mathcal{A}
be sets of ordinals, with
\mathcal{B}^{c}=\mathcal{A}\setminus\mathcal{B}
, and let
\left\langle h_{\zeta},b_{\zeta}\mid\zeta\in\mathcal{A}\right\rangle
be a sequence of cofinal increasing cardinal functions such that
h_{\zeta}\leq^{*}b_{\zeta}
for all
\zeta\in\mathcal{A}
. Let
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}=\prod^{\leq\kappa}_{\zeta\in\mathcal{A}}\mathbb{Q}_{\zeta}
and let
G
be
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
-generic over
\mathbf{V}\vDash\text{``}\,2^{\kappa}=\kappa^{+}\,\text{''}
. If
h^{\prime}\leq^{*}b^{\prime}\in{}^{\kappa}\kappa
are cofinal increasing cardinal functions such that
\left(\sup_{\zeta\in\mathcal{B}^{c}}\left|\mathrm{Loc}_{\leq\alpha}^{b_{\zeta},h_{\zeta}}\right|\right)^{|\alpha|}<h^{\prime}(\alpha)
for almost all
\alpha\in\kappa
, then for each
f\in(\prod b^{\prime})^{\mathbf{V}[G]}
there exists
\varphi\in(\mathrm{Loc}_{\kappa}^{b^{\prime},h^{\prime}})^{\mathbf{V}[G\restriction\mathcal{B}]}
and such that
f\in^{*}\varphi
.

The proof is essentially that of
5.33
. Let us assume that
f\in\mathbf{V}[G]\setminus\mathbf{V}[G\restriction\mathcal{B}]
, since the lemma would be trivially true otherwise. We also assume
\alpha
is large enough to satisfy the condition on the size of
h^{\prime}(\alpha)
.

Let
\dot{f}
be a
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
-name for
f
and let
q\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{*}
read
\dot{f}
early and assume without loss of generality that
q
is modest. For the sake of brevity, let us write
\mathcal{Z}_{\downarrow\alpha}^{q}=\bigcup_{\xi\leq\alpha}\mathcal{Z}^{q}(\xi)
. Note that:

\displaystyle|\mathrm{poss}(q,{\leq}\alpha)|

\displaystyle=\textstyle\prod_{\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}_{\downarrow\alpha}^{q}}\left|\mathrm{Lev}_{\alpha+1}(q(\zeta))\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left(\textstyle\sup_{\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}_{\downarrow\alpha}^{q}}(|\mathrm{Lev}_{\alpha+1}(q(\zeta))|)\right)^{\left|\mathcal{Z}_{\downarrow\alpha}^{q}\right|}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\textstyle\sup_{\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}_{\downarrow\alpha}^{q}}(|\mathrm{Lev}_{\alpha+1}(q(\zeta))|)\right)^{\left|\alpha\right|}

Here the last inequality follows from
q
being modest, which implies that
|\mathcal{Z}_{\downarrow\alpha}^{q}|\leq\alpha
. Since we wish to construct a name
\dot{\varphi}
for some
\varphi\in\mathbf{V}[G\restriction\mathcal{B}]
, we see that
\varphi
is completely decided by the part of the support in
\mathcal{B}
, and thus we may use the part of the support in
\mathcal{B}^{c}
freely to restrict the range of possible values for
f\in\mathbf{V}[G]
in order to make sure that
q\Vdash\text{``}\,\dot{f}\in^{*}\varphi\,\text{''}
, as we did in
5.33
. If we restrict our attention to
\mathcal{B}^{c}
, then we see that

\displaystyle\left(\textstyle\sup_{\zeta\in\mathcal{B}^{c}\cap\mathcal{Z}_{\downarrow\alpha}^{q}}(|\mathrm{Lev}_{\alpha+1}(q(\zeta))|)\right)^{\left|\alpha\right|}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\textstyle\sup_{\zeta\in\mathcal{B}^{c}}(|\mathrm{Lev}_{\alpha+1}(q(\zeta))|)\right)^{\left|\alpha\right|}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\textstyle\sup_{\zeta\in\mathcal{B}^{c}}\left|\mathrm{Loc}_{\leq\alpha}^{b_{\zeta},h_{\zeta}}\right|\right)^{\left|\alpha\right|}<h^{\prime}(\alpha)

This set of possibilities is small enough to define
\varphi\in(\mathrm{Loc}_{\kappa}^{b^{\prime},h^{\prime}})^{\mathbf{V}[G\restriction\mathcal{B}]}
. To be precise, we construct a sequence of names
\langle\dot{B}_{\alpha}\mid\alpha\in\kappa\rangle
for sets
B_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{V}[G\restriction\mathcal{B}]
such that
\mathbf{V}[G\restriction\mathcal{B}]\vDash\text{``}\,|B_{\alpha}|<h^{\prime}(\alpha)\,\text{''}
and such that
q\Vdash\text{``}\,\dot{f}(\alpha)\in\dot{B}_{\alpha}\,\text{''}
.

Since
q
reads
\dot{f}
early, if
\eta\in\mathrm{poss}(q,{\leq}\alpha)
, then let
\gamma_{\eta}\in\kappa
be such that
\eta\land q\Vdash\text{``}\,\dot{f}(\alpha)=\gamma_{\eta}\,\text{''}
.

Given
\eta_{\mathcal{B}}\in\mathrm{poss}(q\restriction\mathcal{B},{\leq}\alpha)
let
Y(\eta_{\mathcal{B}})=\left\{\gamma_{\eta}\mid\eta\in\mathrm{poss}(q,{\leq}\alpha)\text{ and }\eta\restriction\mathcal{B}=\eta_{\mathcal{B}}\right\}
. Now we define the name
\dot{B}_{\alpha}=\left\{\left\langle Y(\eta_{\mathcal{B}}),\eta_{\mathcal{B}}\land q\right\rangle\mid\eta_{\mathcal{B}}\in\mathrm{poss}(q\restriction\mathcal{B},{\leq}\alpha)\right\}
. Since the elements of
Y(\eta_{\mathcal{B}})
are only dependent on the domain
\mathcal{B}^{c}
, it follows by the arithmetic from above that
q\Vdash\text{``}\,|\dot{B}_{\alpha}|<h^{\prime}(\alpha)\,\text{''}
, and thus if
\dot{\varphi}
names a slalom such that
q\Vdash\text{``}\,\dot{\varphi}(\alpha)=\dot{B}_{\alpha}\,\text{''}
, then
q\Vdash\text{``}\,\dot{f}\in^{*}\dot{\varphi}\in\mathrm{Loc}_{\kappa}^{b^{\prime},h^{\prime}}\,\text{''}
.
∎

Corollary 5.44

Let
\mathcal{B}\subseteq\mathcal{A}
be sets of ordinals, with
\mathcal{B}^{c}=\mathcal{A}\setminus\mathcal{B}
, and let
\left\langle h_{\zeta},b_{\zeta}\mid\zeta\in\mathcal{A}\right\rangle
be a sequence of cofinal increasing cardinal functions such that
h_{\zeta}\leq^{*}b_{\zeta}
for all
\zeta\in\mathcal{A}
. Let
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}=\prod^{\leq\kappa}_{\zeta\in\mathcal{A}}\mathbb{Q}_{\zeta}
and let
G
be
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
-generic over
\mathbf{V}
. If
h^{\prime},b^{\prime},\tilde{h},\tilde{b}\in{}^{\kappa}\kappa
are cofinal increasing cardinal functions such that
\left(\sup_{\zeta\in\mathcal{B}^{c}}\left|\mathrm{Loc}_{\leq\alpha}^{b_{\zeta},h_{\zeta}}\right|\right)^{|\alpha|}<h^{\prime}(\alpha)\leq\tilde{b}(\alpha)^{\tilde{h}(\alpha)}\leq b^{\prime}(\alpha)
and
h^{\prime}(\alpha)\cdot\tilde{h}(\alpha)<\tilde{b}(\alpha)
for almost all
\alpha\in\kappa
, then if
\psi\in(\mathrm{Loc}_{\kappa}^{\tilde{b},\tilde{h}})^{\mathbf{V}[G]}
there exists
g\in(\prod\tilde{b})^{\mathbf{V}[G\restriction\mathcal{B}]}
such that
g\mathrel{\cancel{\in^{\infty}}}\psi
.

This follows from
5.43
, similar to how
5.34
follows from
5.33
.
∎

Theorem 5.45

There exists a family of parameters
\left\langle h_{\gamma},b_{\gamma}\mid\gamma\in\kappa\right\rangle
such that for any finite
\gamma_{n}<\dots<\gamma_{0}<\kappa
and cardinals
\kappa^{+}=\lambda_{0}<\dots<\lambda_{n}
with
\mathrm{cf}(\lambda_{i})>\kappa
for each
i\in[0,n]
, there exists a forcing extension where
\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b_{\gamma_{i}},h_{\gamma_{i}}}(\mathrel{\cancel{\ni^{\infty}}})=\lambda_{i}
for each
i\in[0,n]
.

Let
h_{0}\in{}^{\kappa}\kappa
be a cofinal increasing cardinal functions such that
h_{0}(\alpha)\geq|\alpha|
for all
\alpha\in\kappa
. For each
\gamma\in\kappa
define
h_{\gamma},b_{\gamma}
recursively as follows: let
h_{\gamma+1}(\alpha)=b_{\gamma}(\alpha)^{h_{\gamma}(\alpha)}
and if
\gamma
is limit, let
h_{\gamma}(\alpha)=\sup_{\xi\in\gamma}h_{\xi}(\alpha)
, and finally let
b_{\gamma}(\alpha)=2^{h_{\gamma}(\alpha)}
for all
\gamma\in\kappa
.

Now let
\kappa^{+}=\lambda_{0}<\lambda_{1}<\dots<\lambda_{n}
be a finite sequence of regular cardinals and let
\gamma_{0}>\dots>\gamma_{n}
be a decreasing sequence of ordinals. Let
A_{1},\dots,A_{n}
be disjoint sets of ordinals such that
|A_{i}|=\lambda_{i}
for each
i\in[1,n]
, and let
\mathcal{A}=\bigcup_{i\in[1,n]}A_{i}
. Now for each
\zeta\in\mathcal{A}
let
h_{\zeta}^{\prime}=h_{\gamma_{i}}
and
b_{\zeta}^{\prime}=b_{\gamma_{i}}
iff
\zeta\in A_{i}
, and let
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}=\prod^{\leq\kappa}_{\zeta\in\mathcal{A}}\mathbb{Q}_{h_{\zeta}^{\prime},b_{\zeta}^{\prime}}
. Let
G
be
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
-generic over
\mathbf{V}
and assume that
\mathbf{V}\vDash\text{``}\,2^{\kappa}=\kappa^{+}\,\text{''}
.

Fix
1\leq i\leq n
. From
5.43
it follows that
\mathbf{V}[G\restriction A_{i}]\vDash\text{``}\,\lambda_{i}\leq\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b_{\gamma_{i}},h_{\gamma_{i}}}(\mathrel{\cancel{\ni^{\infty}}})\,\text{''}
. If we let
\mathcal{B}=\bigcup_{i\in[1,i]}A_{i}
, then also
|\mathcal{B}|=\lambda_{i}
and consequently
\mathbf{V}[G\restriction\mathcal{B}]\vDash\text{``}\,2^{\kappa}=\lambda_{i}\,\text{''}
. If
i<j\leq n
, then
h_{\gamma_{j}},b_{\gamma_{j}}
are much smaller than
h_{\gamma_{i}},b_{\gamma_{i}}
, and thus by
5.44
we see that
(\prod b_{\gamma_{i}})^{\mathbf{V}[G\restriction\mathcal{B}]}
forms a witness to prove that
\mathbf{V}[G]\vDash\text{``}\,\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b_{\gamma_{i}},h_{\gamma_{i}}}(\mathrel{\cancel{\ni^{\infty}}})\leq\lambda_{i}\,\text{''}
.
∎

6. Discussion

Throughout this article we have mentioned several open questions. In this section we will collect these open questions.

Firstly, we have several open questions regarding the parameters for which the cardinal characteristics on the
\mathfrak{d}
-side become trivial, in the sense that they will be equal to
2^{\kappa}
. We do have full characterisations of the trivial parameters for the cardinals on the
\mathfrak{b}
-side with
3.35
,
3.3
and
3.17
, and by duality it is a reasonable conjecture that the cardinals on the
\mathfrak{d}
-side will be trivial under the same conditions.

Question 6.1

Assume case (ii) of
3.3
. Can we prove that
\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b}(\leq^{*})=2^{\kappa}
?

Question 6.2

Assume case (ii) of
3.17
and that there does not exist an almost disjoint family
\mathcal{A}\subseteq\prod b
of functions with
|\mathcal{A}|=2^{\kappa}
. Can we prove that
\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b,h}(\in^{*})=2^{\kappa}
? Specifically, if
b
is continuous on a club set, is
\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b,h}(\in^{*})<2^{\kappa}
consistent?

Question 6.3

Assume case (i) or (ii) of
3.35
. Can we prove that
\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b,h}(\mathrel{\cancel{\ni^{\infty}}})=2^{\kappa}
?

Question 6.4

Assume case (i) or (ii) of
3.42
. Can we prove that
\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b}(\mathrel{\cancel{=^{\infty}}})=2^{\kappa}
?

Next there are several open questions regarding consistency of strict relations. As we saw, the
\kappa
-Hechler models show that it is consistent that
\mathrm{add}(\mathcal{M}_{\kappa})
is strictly larger than
\mathfrak{sup}_{\kappa}(\mathrel{\cancel{=^{\infty}}})
, and that
\mathrm{cof}(\mathcal{M}_{\kappa})
can be strictly smaller than
\mathfrak{inf}_{\kappa}(\mathrel{\cancel{=^{\infty}}})
.

It is, however, possible (relative to some large cardinal assumptions) to add
b
-dominating
\kappa
-reals, that is, functions
f\in\prod b
such that
f\geq^{*}g
for all
g\in\prod b
from the ground model, without adding dominating
\kappa
-reals. In fact, the bounded
\kappa
-Hechler forcing
\mathbb{D}_{\kappa}^{b}(\kappa)
from
3.8
will not add any new
\kappa
-real
f\in{}^{\kappa}\kappa
such that
f
dominates all ground model functions in
{}^{\kappa}\kappa
when
\kappa
is weakly compact. See for example
[
She20
]
, where Shelah constructs a model where
\mathrm{cov}(\mathcal{M}_{\kappa})<\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}(\leq^{*})
, under the assumption of a supercompact cardinal.

This makes the following question natural:

Question 6.5

Is it consistent that
\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b}(\leq^{*})<\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}(\leq^{*})
for all
b\in{}^{\kappa}\kappa
? Is it consistent that
\mathfrak{b}_{\kappa}(\leq^{*})<\mathfrak{b}_{\kappa}^{b}(\leq^{*})
for all
b\in{}^{\kappa}\kappa
?

We can ask the same question for the relation between bounded and unbounded localisation cardinals. That is, for a suitable function
h\in{}^{\kappa}\kappa
, can we separate cardinals of the form
\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b,h}(\in^{*})
from the cardinal
\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{h}(\in^{*})
as defined on the unbounded generalised Baire space. Shelah’s model from
[
She20
]
may be a promising candidate to consider for the
\mathfrak{d}
-side.

Question 6.6

Is it consistent that
\mathfrak{sup}_{\kappa}^{h}(\in^{*})<\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{h}(\in^{*})
?
Is it consistent that
\mathfrak{b}_{\kappa}^{h}(\in^{*})<\mathfrak{inf}_{\kappa}^{h}(\in^{*})
?

Finally, we have shown that it is consistent that there is a family of
\kappa^{+}
many parameters
h_{\xi},b_{\xi}
, such that the corresponding cardinals of the form
\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b_{\xi},h_{\xi}}(\in^{*})
are mutually distinct, and that there exists a family of
\kappa
many parameters
h_{\xi},b_{\xi}
such that for any finite set of cardinals
\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b_{\xi},h_{\xi}}(\mathrel{\cancel{\ni^{\infty}}})
there is a model in which they are mutually distinct. Classically we know that there exists a model in which there are
2^{\aleph_{0}}
many distinct cardinals of each of the forms
\mathfrak{d}_{\omega}^{b,h}(\in^{*})
,
\mathfrak{b}_{\omega}^{b,h}(\in^{*})
,
\mathfrak{d}_{\omega}^{b,h}(\mathrel{\cancel{\ni^{\infty}}})
and
\mathfrak{b}_{\omega}^{b,h}(\mathrel{\cancel{\ni^{\infty}}})
, as described in
[
CKM21
]
.

Question 6.7

Can there consistently be
2^{\kappa}
many different cardinalities of the form
\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b,h}(\in^{*})
?

Question 6.8

Can there consistently be infinitely many different cardinalities of the form
\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa}^{b,h}(\mathrel{\cancel{\ni^{\infty}}})
?

Moreover, the type of forcing notions we used to separate (anti)localisation cardinals, was a
{\leq}\kappa
-support product of perfect tree forcing notions. This method cannot be used to obtain dual models, such as we did with
\kappa
-Hechler forcing, and hence we do not have any separation of cardinals of the form
\mathfrak{b}_{\kappa}^{b,h}(\in^{*})
and
\mathfrak{b}_{\kappa}^{b,h}(\mathrel{\cancel{\ni^{\infty}}})
.

Separating such cardinals could be done in the classical context with a Laver-type forcing notion. However, in the generalised context, Laver-type forcing notions behave quite differently. For example, most reasonable generalisations of Laver forcing add
\kappa
-Cohen reals, see
[
KKLW22
]
. Another method would be to use an appropriate version of localisation forcing, but we would need a preservation theorem to show that
\mathfrak{b}_{\kappa}^{b,h}(\in^{*})
remains small for certain parameters. The preservation of properties of forcing notions under finite or countable support forcing notions is usually unproblematic in the classical case, if proper forcing notions are considered, but generalisations of such preservation theorems are not known for
{<}\kappa
- or
{\leq}\kappa
-support iterations of forcing notions that add
\kappa
-reals.

In conclusion, we currently do not know how to separate even two cardinals of the form
\mathfrak{b}_{\kappa}^{b,h}(\in^{*})
. For the same reason, this question is also open for cardinals of the form
\mathfrak{b}_{\kappa}^{h}(\in^{*})
, see for example Question 71 and the discussion of section 5.2 in
[
BBTFM18
]
.

Question 6.9

Do there exist nontrivial parameters
b,h,b^{\prime},h^{\prime}\in{}^{\kappa}\kappa
such that
\mathfrak{b}_{\kappa}^{b,h}(\in^{*})<\mathfrak{b}_{\kappa}^{b^{\prime},h^{\prime}}(\in^{*})
is consistent? Or such that
\mathfrak{b}_{\kappa}^{b,h}(\mathrel{\cancel{\ni^{\infty}}})<\mathfrak{b}_{\kappa}^{b^{\prime},h^{\prime}}(\mathrel{\cancel{\ni^{\infty}}})
is consistent?

*
